# Nexus Operative — Main Launch Drive: v1.0

## v0.203.0 exact fee capture upper bound

The private exact response-bundle review now rejects an explicitly referenced retained fee whose full timestamp is later than the replay-validated retained activity collection completion instant. The existing lower bound against the referenced fill remains in force. Date-only fees remain temporally unverified without an invented timezone, time of day or posting instant.

The public result adds only a boolean local upper-bound-check marker; aggregate timestamped/date-only counts and the existing digest remain the only fee-link detail. This does not attest either clock or source, prove semantic fee links, execution/fee completeness or posting, reconcile holds or spendable funds, establish account ownership or authorize routing. `fee_execution_links_verified` remains false. No UI control or modifiable parameter changed; the seven beginner tabs, black/bright-purple bottom-right help, OFF permissions, bounded risk/account checks, fail-closed exits and independent emergency stop are unchanged.

## v0.201.0 shared-order retained-fill temporal partition

Continued from exact verified branch head `d19a08275ce17560eff9b7f3bc971d4e37c26989` (v0.200.0). Its public security/history/Gitleaks run 39660190612, safety/regression/evidence/browser run 39660190667 and Windows regression/EXE/installer run 39660190638 passed. These results do not transfer to the new source.

The private exact response-bundle review now partitions every retained fill for an order visible in both the earlier account inventory and later activity order snapshot. Retained fills at or below the earlier cumulative quantity must occur no later than the earlier order update. Fills above it remain subject to exact contiguous cumulative progression, `(earlier update, later update]` event time and weighted-average agreement. A late fill can no longer be silently accepted as already-accounted history.

Only aggregate historical/retained counts and a SHA256 partition digest are public. This does not attest the provider, timestamps, source/activity completeness, atomicity, account ownership, execution/fee completeness, fee posting/linkage, holds or spendable funds. No UI, credential, network, permission, risk, emergency, funding or trading path changed. Seven beginner tabs, black/bright-purple bottom-right help, OFF defaults and fail-closed exits remain intact.

The first complete run exposed a pre-existing nondeterministic privacy-fixture assertion after 3,536 passes: it searched opaque SQLite/hash bytes for the common text `1000`. The corrected fixture checks decoded exact scalars for that ordinary balance while retaining raw-byte checks for distinctive private values. This is test-evidence reliability only; runtime journal schemas, deadlines and safety controls are unchanged.

Next: authenticated broker-paper source/activity/correction evidence, execution and fee completeness, fee posting, cash/position/hold/spendable-funds reconciliation and monitored provider-paper lifecycle, followed by sustained genuine-market trials and native profile/ACL/power-loss acceptance. Funding/live/cloud, independent review, owner approval and 1.0 remain separate gates. Rough implementation remains about 40% (30–45%, low confidence), with 0/9 accepted stages.

Final local v0.201.0 validation passed 3,538 tests with one expected non-Windows skip and one existing warning; the final release-focused set passed 176 tests, and the corrected aggregate fixture passed 100 repeated runs. Sixteen profiles and saved-evidence verification matched 357 source files / `4b965469e4b18aeaa877d1c2efc98cc8d59486945b8528f96ffdc1e14e12d579`; report SHA256 is `6ed4d773094538876d3ae03bb955f12eeaa673607b16b2be49a9421d191a5b6c`. ## v0.199.0 exact response-bundle fill-delta binding

For each order visible in both adjacent exact response components whose cumulative fill increases, require exact retained FILL activities to form a gap-free chain from the earlier quantity to the later quantity. Their event times must fall strictly after the earlier update and no later than the later update. Earlier filled notional plus every new exact fill quantity-times-price must reproduce the later weighted average fill price. Output adds only aggregate delta/activity counts and one digest; private identities, quantities, prices and timestamps remain outside public evidence.

This strengthens local execution consistency but does not attest the provider, prove complete received activity, make sequential reads atomic, link or post fees, reconcile holds or spendable funds, establish account ownership, or authorize routing. No UI control or editable parameter changed. Seven beginner tabs, black/bright-purple bottom-right help, OFF permissions, risk/account gates, fail-closed exits and independent emergency stop are unchanged.

## v0.198.0 exact response-bundle order-progress binding

The private exact-response bundle now requires every order shared by the earlier account-inventory response and later retained activity snapshot to progress monotonically. Update time and cumulative filled quantity cannot regress, a changed state cannot reuse the same update instant, unchanged cumulative quantity cannot change its average fill price, and status must follow the existing conservative transition graph. The report exposes only an aggregate transition count and digest.

This closes a local sequential-state integrity gap only. It does not make the reads atomic, prove provider/source/received-activity coverage, establish account ownership, verify execution or fee completeness, reconcile holds or spendable funds, or authorize routing. Non-overlapping orders remain allowed. Engagement stays OFF by default; limits, fail-closed exits, the independent emergency stop, seven-tab beginner UI and black/bright-purple bottom-right help are unchanged.

## v0.190.0 reviewed account-pacing recovery

The account-scoped pacing journal now retains a content-free recovery event after an operator explicitly reviews an exact blocked AUTH_DENIED, REVIEW_REQUIRED or INTERRUPTED_UNKNOWN tail. Account interruptions remain review-blocked even after the full-day cooldown. The event requires the exact current head/account, live local ownership and separate reviewed-block/uncertainty confirmations. It preserves the original outcome, operation history, cooldown and session count and does not reserve or perform another read.

The installed/source `--paper-tool account-recovery` entry point refuses missing, stale, foreign or unconfirmed inputs without creating a pacing journal or exposing private arguments. **Primary objective:** finish, validate and package a reliable, easy-to-use Windows Nexus Operative v1.0.0. **Secondary parallel objective:** expand the researched opportunity universe globally, as documented in [EXPANSION_INITIATIVE.md](EXPANSION_INITIATIVE.md). Breadth must not delay closure of primary readiness defects.

## Core automated-execution ambition

Nexus is intended to evaluate public information, rank independently eligible opportunities, and eventually manage orders more quickly and consistently than manual human operation. This requires bounded machine response time, not a promise of outperforming prices, undercutting other traders, guaranteed fills, or profit. Exchange rates, market data latency, network conditions, queue priority, adverse selection, fees, API rate limits, and order-book depth determine actual outcomes.

### Critical path to a usable v1.0
1. **Green Windows build and recovery** — reproducible unit/integration tests, well-formed PORTAL, installer smoke test, durable paper data, safe halt/restart.
2. **Wide but disciplined data observation** — authenticated provenance of public feeds, bounded caches, timestamp alignment, missing-data veto and multi-source checks. Scan many markets without flooding APIs.
3. **Deterministic low-latency signal-to-decision pipeline** — asynchronous bounded event queue, deduplication, monotonic timing, stale-signal expiry, fixed risk budgets and predictable overload behavior. Decisions remain research/paper-only until separate approval.
4. **Execution lifecycle simulator** — independent order-intent IDs, simulated acknowledgment / partial fill / cancellation / rejection, idempotency, conservative spread+fee+slippage, and durable ledger reconciliation. Reject any unverified or stale input.
5. **Latency and capacity validation** — p50/p95/p99 local decision delays, burst handling, simulated exchange delays, worst-case lost/duplicate/out-of-order messages and emergency stop behavior. Local timing is not exchange fill speed.
6. **Scientific market performance evidence** — fixed strategies tested with chronological holdouts, cross-regime/volume stress, survivorship/delisting checks, cost sensitivity and recorded failed candidates.
7. **Autonomous paper forward operation** — scheduled research, simulated position sizing, risk veto and monitoring with no need for constant manual interaction; repeated restarts and interrupted-network drills.
8. **Safety and operational signoff** — protected secrets, explicit provider permissions, independent code/security review, incident response, audited controls, installer and operator documentation.
9. **Explicit release authorization** — preserve 0.x until all v1.0 gates have evidence and the user approves. Any real-money activation separately requires explicit authorization, broker agreements, legal/venue eligibility and certified order routing.

### What is *not* being activated today
No broker account, live funds, exchange placement, real orders, leveraged execution or money movement. Deterministic simulations can be automated without that authorization.

### Timing targets are acceptance tests, not marketing claims
Set local reaction-time budgets for decision and risk evaluation; measure p50/p95/p99 under synthetic event bursts in CI. Do not advertise millisecond market access until true market-data-to-acknowledgment traces demonstrate it, and never claim to be faster than competing trading systems without comparison evidence.

## Standing sprint rule
Each hourly sprint should **first fix CI/build regressions and then complete the largest coherent unmet v1.0 launch gate** with test coverage. Allocate remaining capacity to global expansion as a research/discovery track. Do not increase a feature version just for plans or cosmetic metadata. Do not trade readiness for adding more listings.

## First external execution service decision (planned)
Use **Alpaca paper** as the first third-party broker API adapter: separate paper credentials and endpoint, broker order-ID reconciliation, provider-specific rate limits and asset eligibility. Add an IBKR paper adapter for expanded stocks/FX/options/futures after sessions and entitlements are verified; consider Coinbase Advanced as a separate authenticated crypto execution venue. Until explicit user permission, these adapters stay disconnected; they are not substitutes for independently tested execution safeguards.

The provider-neutral `nexus/order_gateway.py` models unique client order IDs, no-network simulated acknowledgement, partial/full fill events, rejection, cancellation and emergency cancellation. Ordered incremental synthetic fills track explicit fees; duplicate events are idempotent, conflicting replays and overfills are rejected, and cancellation preserves partial fills. The PORTAL uses a transactional SQLite command journal (`nexus/durable_orders.py`) to replay and validate offline state after restart, including IDs, fills, fees, cancellations and halt/reset. Lifecycle events are not exposed as a public API or wired into the portfolio ledger. Quantity/price-based portfolio reconciliation, price/spread/slippage modeling and broker-confirmed fills remain unfinished. It does not manage real capital or hold actual assets.

## v0.49: 30 local decisions/second capacity target
The bounded no-network `nexus/decision_lane.py` benchmark tests pure in-process synthetic research decisions. It reports local throughput, p50/p95/p99 timing, staleness and queue overflow, and **no broker orders or fills**. The user desires roughly 30 or more completed market moves per second in the long run; do not conflate this with internal processing. Provider rate limits, costs, available liquidity, matching engine processing, order acknowledgments and market impact require separate end-to-end validation. Build streaming feeds, durable lifecycle and venue-aware pacing before claiming exchange throughput. Alpaca Trading API generally 200 requests/min/account by default; IBKR TWS commonly up to 50 client messages/second; IBKR Web API default 10 requests/sec; verify applicable conditions/headers with actual provider docs and entitlements.


## Release readiness review — v0.51.0

These are evidence-based stages, not a percent-complete estimate or release date.

| Stage | Current evidence | Remaining acceptance work |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Windows foundation | Automated test, EXE startup and installer workflows exist | Clean-machine install/upgrade/uninstall and recovery drills; record artifact checksums |
| Tier authority | Terrestrial/Solar/Universal entry controls and exit behavior have regression coverage | Verify them throughout the autonomous buyer/seller loop |
| Order lifecycle | Offline partial/full fills, fees, rejection, idempotency and cancellation tests | Price/quantity fills with realistic execution costs and expiry |
| Durable recovery | SQLite transactions preserve commands; replay validates transitions; halt survives restart | Ledger linkage and reconciliation; crash/process-kill drills; retention/checkpoint design |
| Low-latency execution | Bounded local decision benchmark | Measure durable end-to-end paper path and venue pacing, not just decision throughput |
| Data and AI research | Provenance, data checks, model adapters and holdout/stress modules exist | Independent audits and recorded provider/failure/cost evidence |
| Paper autonomy | Paper portfolio and risk gates exist | Integrated unattended forward runs, monitored restarts and network-failure exercises |
| Release signoff | Release gates documented | Independent security/risk review, operator runbook and owner acceptance |

### Next coherent milestones, in order
1. Add explicit simulated execution price/quantity to lifecycle fills and atomically reconcile each fill with the paper ledger, including duplicate delivery and partial cancellations. Never infer holdings from reference notionals.
2. Replace full command replay on every write with validated checkpoints/indexed state, preserving restart correctness and audit history. Benchmark durable latency and test controlled process interruption.
3. Join streaming observations, tier permissions, risk vetoes, lifecycle accounting and monitoring into an unattended paper loop. Keep 5-second broad refresh, 15-second ranking and top-30 AI candidates with adaptive acceleration.
4. Implement the Alpaca paper adapter only within explicitly authorized paper scope; verify IDs, provider pacing, disconnected recovery and credentials. Follow with IBKR paper and Coinbase research as separate milestones.
5. Collect forward-run and research evidence, complete installer/recovery/security acceptance, then request 1.0 release authorization.

### Recovery operation and limits
`GET /api/execution/recovery` returns validated offline order state and an explicit `portfolio_reconciled: false`. The SQLite journal is stored under `NEXUS_PORTABLE_DATA` (default `~/.nexus-operative`). Writes acknowledge only after transaction commit; invalid transitions roll back. Independent writers serialize through SQLite. Startup restores the halt latch and stays paused; invalid recovery fails closed. Reset cannot revive terminal orders.

The journal is bounded to 10,000 state-changing commands, reserving 1,001 slots from ordinary intake for control operations. Repeated idempotent requests do not consume slots. Full replay is conservative and is **not yet evidence of the desired durable throughput**. Recovery validates lifecycle structure, not malicious database rewriting or actual ownership. Existing in-memory orders from older running processes cannot be recovered retroactively. Global market expansion stays secondary to these launch blockers.

## v0.52.0 — priced synthetic execution accounting

The durable gateway now supports internal `initialize_account` and `simulate_priced_fill` commands. Explicit quantity, price and fee establish reference notional, cash debit/credit and weighted acquisition cost. Partial buys accumulate quantity; partial sells reduce quantity without shorting. Lifecycle transitions and derived accounting share one committed command, so failed writes cannot acknowledge a fill and duplicate commands cannot change balances twice. Restart reconstructs both from committed commands.

This is a **separate synthetic execution account**, reported under `execution_account` by the recovery endpoint. It does not update or combine with the existing public-quote paper portfolio. That existing portfolio's reconstruction rules currently assume whole-position operations; extending it to partial fills needs its own migration and compatibility review. `portfolio_reconciled` therefore remains false. Historical fills without price/quantity stay explicitly unreconciled and never create invented holdings. No priced-fill mutation endpoint or automated trading loop is exposed.

Validation covers partial buys/sells, restart, concurrent duplicate delivery, conflicting execution details, overfills, overselling, insufficient cash, invalid numbers and failed-commit rollback. Next: unify the two accounting paths with compatible position-event reconstruction and tier/risk enforcement, then measure durable throughput and process-kill recovery. The internal priced-fill helper alone does not satisfy the complete portfolio reconciliation gate.

## v0.53.0 — linked reconciliation, crash recovery and bundled roadmap

The current continuation contract is [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md), generated from `nexus/release_roadmap.py`. PORTAL provides a read-only Roadmap to 1.0 view and `GET /api/roadmap`. `AGENTS.md` tells future development sessions where to resume. These are bundled source references; no runtime roadmap editing is exposed. Earlier sections above are historical milestone records, not current status overrides.

An explicit internal `link_portfolio()` operation can now link a new execution account to a new or unused matching paper ledger. Existing traded accounts are rejected rather than auto-merged. Linked priced fills write the execution command and paper-ledger details in one SQLite attached-database transaction with FULL synchronization and rollback journals. The governor reconstructs partial buys/sells, verifies fill price/quantity/fee against cash deltas, and includes execution details in transaction seals. Unpriced fills cannot enter linked accounts.

Linked entry fills require their individual risk tier to be engaged and within its intent cap. Existing portfolio position and aggregate exposure limits remain active. Disengaged tiers still allow exits and duplicate replay. Quote-driven manual writes are blocked after explicit linking to avoid competing accounting paths. Recovery compares the reconstructed execution account with the sealed paper ledger before reporting `portfolio_reconciled: true`; this remains local simulation evidence only.

Completed component checks include partial fills and partial cancellation, independent cash/position detail checks, duplicate replay after tier disengagement, two-database rollback on failed commit, and a subprocess forcibly terminated after portfolio writes but before command insertion/commit. Restart rolls back both databases; retry records one fill. Emergency halt can still persist despite a portfolio discrepancy; reset remains vetoed until integrity is restored.

Remaining M4 acceptance: wider spread/slippage/expiry scenarios, repeated interruption stress, independent migration/reconciliation review, and integration into the unattended loop. Full replay remains a capacity bottleneck; M5 checkpoints/retention and durable p50/p95/p99 measurement remain next. No public priced-fill/link mutation endpoint or external broker connection was added.

## v0.54.0 — bounded synthetic quote execution and expiry

The internal quote-fill model uses explicit synthetic bid/ask, available quantity and observation/evaluation timestamps. Buys use ask plus adverse slippage; sells use bid minus adverse slippage, with explicit per-fill fees. Inputs must be finite, positive where applicable, no older than two seconds, uncrossed and within a 150 bps spread bound. Fee/slippage assumptions are bounded at 100 bps each. These are test assumptions, not measured depth, queue priority, market latency or actual fills.

Execution terms are immutable once set (maximum one-hour lifetime) and require an unfilled order. Timed orders cannot bypass the quote path through raw reference fills or direct priced fills. Evaluations cannot precede order creation or regress behind earlier modeled evaluations. Equivalent numeric inputs replay idempotently; conflicting quote/cost assumptions are rejected.

Expiry is a durable terminal transition at the deadline. It preserves any existing partial quantity, fees and cash accounting. Single-order and bounded batch expiry share the journal transaction; repeated expiry is idempotent, restart preserves it and reset does not reopen expired orders. Linked quote fills use the same atomic ledger bridge, risk-tier entry checks and exposure governor. Disengaged tiers still permit modeled exits and historical duplicate replay.

The roadmap UTF-8 read test was corrected after Windows CI detected locale-dependent decoding. No broker/network integration or public mutation endpoint was added. Next critical path: validated checkpoints/retention and full durable-path latency measurements, then the integrated unattended paper loop. M4 remains partial pending extended stress and independent acceptance.

## v0.55.0 — validated warm checkpoints and durable latency diagnostic

The journal now saves a JSON checkpoint every 64 state-changing commands in the same transaction as the command and any linked ledger writes. It retains the two latest checkpoint snapshots and all command history. Checkpoint write failure rolls back the command/fill as well. No pickle, automatic history deletion or journal-cap bypass is used.

A fresh process validates the saved state through full prefix replay. A bounded four-entry process cache subsequently restores that verified state only when the exact snapshot and retained command-prefix digest still match. Tail commands are replayed normally; changed prefix payloads, invalid sequence bounds or inconsistent snapshots fail closed. Checkpoints speed warm reconstruction; cold recovery still performs full prefix replay and every warm check still reads/hashes retained prefix payloads. Local checksums cannot prevent coordinated database rewrites.

Tests cover cold/warm equivalence to full replay, tail processing, bounded snapshot retention, modified snapshot/history rejection, rollback of linked fills when checkpoint insertion fails, and forced process death during checkpoint insertion. The existing journal limit remains 10,000 commands, with ordinary intake reserving 1,001 control slots. Safe long-run archival still needs design and acceptance evidence.

PORTAL offers an explicit disposable-account durability benchmark (`POST /api/execution/durable-benchmark`, 1–30 cycles, default 16). It compares full replay with checkpoints across submit, timed quote fill and partial cancellation, and reports local commit-return p50/p95/p99, command throughput and cold/warm recovery. Treatment order is fixed and this is a diagnostic, not a statistically controlled speedup claim. It excludes the linked portfolio, live tier validation, network, broker acknowledgments and actual fills. It neither changes application accounts nor validates the desired market-moves-per-second target.

Next: linked-ledger/tier-inclusive measurement, bounded sustained-load handling and journal archival, followed by unattended buyer/hold/seller integration. The read-only roadmap marks M5 partial, not complete.

## v0.56.0 — linked measurement and capacity admission diagnostics

The disposable benchmark now compares four treatments: gateway-only/full replay, gateway-only/checkpoint, linked-ledger/full replay, and linked-ledger/checkpoint. Linked treatments execute the real per-tier entry checks, integrity governor and atomic fill-to-ledger reconciliation against separate disposable accounts. Buy/exit cycles end in partial cancellation with no remaining holdings. The measurements still exclude feed acquisition, strategy evaluation, event-queue delays, network, broker acknowledgments and actual fills. Fixed treatment order and short runs are diagnostics, not statistically controlled performance claims.

Disposable tier/settings databases are scoped through a ContextVar and reset in a finally block. The benchmark never changes the process environment, real account configuration or user tier permissions. Cross-thread isolation and exception-reset tests cover this scope. Settings and permission connections now close explicitly, including before Windows temporary-directory cleanup.

Execution recovery includes a read-only capacity snapshot, also viewable in PORTAL. It reports command/intention counts and limits, ordinary writes remaining, reserved control capacity and admission reasons. New entries stop at their bounded journal or historical-ID limits. Tests demonstrate that duplicates still replay without consuming commands, while remaining cancellation, expiry and halt operations can use control slots. Cancelled order IDs remain reserved for idempotency. No automatic archival, deletion or cap bypass was added.

Next: sustained-load and concurrent admission testing, safe audit-preserving journal archival, and integration of the monitored unattended paper loop. M5 remains partial; temporary throughput figures do not establish real market execution speed.

## v0.57.0 — explicit archival, bounded epochs and grouped operation drills

Completed orders can now be logically archived through an explicit maintenance operation while the execution gateway and PORTAL are halted and paused. A reconciled, flat account is required; holdings are never liquidated by maintenance. Active IDs move into retained state with their full lifecycle events, priced executions, quote assumptions and immutable terms. Each batch records its IDs, journal sequence boundary and SHA-256 evidence digest. Old submissions and fill deliveries still replay idempotently; conflicting reuse remains rejected. Maintenance never resumes PORTAL.

All commands remain in the same SQLite journal with uninterrupted global sequence numbers. Only the active admission epoch resets, transactionally with archival. The limits are now explicit and separate: 10,000 commands per active epoch (1,001 reserved from normal intake for control operations), 100,000 retained commands, 1,000 active IDs, 10,000 archived IDs and 100 archive batches. Reaching retained-history limits still stops intake; this is bounded continuity, not indefinite operation or physical compaction. There is no automatic deletion or archival. The previous checkpoint schema remains readable by normalizing absent archival fields before comparing against full prefix replay.

PORTAL includes explicit archival maintenance and a disposable scripted operation drill. `POST /api/execution/archive-terminal` requires confirmation and halted/paused state; `GET /api/execution/intent/{id}` retrieves active or archived order evidence read-only. `POST /api/execution/operation-drill` accepts 1–24 cycles (default 12) and groups synthetic BUY/HOLD/SELL, a disengaged-entry veto, allowed exit, partial cancellation, halt, archive and gateway-instance reconstruction. Its settings and accounts are disposable; it is not live-data strategy autonomy, market-performance evidence or a network-outage drill.

Tests cover archived ID/fill replay and conflicts, flat/halt requirements, failed archival commit, corrupt sequence boundary, legacy checkpoint compatibility, 24 linked operation epochs and 48 preserved order IDs, plus process death during the archive checkpoint write. That forced death preserves the old halted epoch, commands and accounting on restart. The read-only roadmap carries these completed component checks forward.

Next: physical history offload/scale measurements, bounded scheduling and live-data paper-loop integration, repeated network-failure drills, then sustained forward evidence and independent release acceptance. M5 stays partial and M7 stays open until those broader gates have evidence.

## v0.58.0 — opt-in BTC public-quote paper forward runner

A separate experimental runner can now connect public quote observations to modeled BUY/HOLD/SELL execution through the linked ledger. The read-only observer retains its original no-order behavior. The new runner defaults stopped; explicit session confirmation, active/unhalted PORTAL and compatible linked accounting are required. New entry sessions require an engaged selected tier; existing BTC holdings may resume exit-only monitoring while that tier is disengaged. Fresh accounting may be initialized from the paper budget, but pre-existing traded/unverifiable accounts are not auto-merged. Sessions reject unsupported holdings or outstanding orders requiring review.

Scope is deliberately narrow: BTC-USD polling every five seconds, entry evaluation every 15 seconds, six distinct observations over at least 25 seconds, and a fixed experimental 30 bps momentum entry threshold. Position exits use bid-to-cost-basis moves of ±50 bps or an initial-capital loss threshold. Entries are capped at the stricter of $50, 1% of initial paper capital and existing tier/governor vetoes. Liquidity is assumed at the modeled quantity; adverse slippage and fees still apply. This is not a validated strategy, independently attested feed or real execution model. The broader 30-candidate AI/ranking and adaptive research objectives remain separate unfinished work.

Quotes require matching BTC identity, timezone-aware exchange timestamps, valid public provenance, two-second receive freshness, ten-second exchange freshness, bounded spreads and the feed guard. Duplicate/regressing exchange observations cannot trigger new entries. Three consecutive invalid/unavailable quotes pause the session; storage/integrity/capacity failure also pauses. Initial entry admission reserves an additional order ID and journal writes for an exit. Disengaged tiers continue to allow exits. Stops preserve holdings; they never silently liquidate or restart.

The order sequence is fenced so stop acknowledges after any in-flight local execution finishes. PORTAL pause, emergency halt, halt reset and application shutdown stop the runner. Restart reconstructs accounting but does not restore running permission. Cycle decisions and vetoes are retained in a separate bounded 1,000-row diagnostic log; execution history is not trimmed.

Tests inject timestamped quotes to exercise buy, hold, exit, tier veto, duplicate ticks, stale/provider failures, identity/timezone rejection, stop during fetch, reserved exit capacity, failed start diagnostics, API opt-in, pause/halt and shutdown. No public-feed session was started as part of this implementation. M7 is partial: sustained public-data forward runs, outages, independent strategy evidence and broader scheduling remain required.

## v0.59.0 — forward-session evidence and outage admission hardening

Forward cycles now atomically update a durable session summary and its diagnostic event. Summaries retain started/updated times, decision counters, last event, stored state and the exact policy parameters/fingerprint. Stop, pause/halt/reset and shutdown carry explicit reasons. The 1,000-row rolling cycle log remains diagnostic; up to 1,000 session summaries are retained without automatic deletion, after which new sessions are vetoed. The order journal and sealed portfolio remain the execution/accounting source of truth: session counters are not independently anchored proof of fills.

Read-only session evidence identifies stale heartbeats, previous/interrupted running sessions and a stopped worker without a final persisted record. These findings require review; neither reading evidence nor restarting the process grants running permission. PORTAL includes session-evidence review. `GET /api/paper/forward/sessions` and `GET /api/paper/forward/policies` expose the same diagnostics and fixed-policy catalogue.

`nexus/paper_policy.py` centralizes a versioned immutable experiment definition and SHA-256 parameter fingerprint. Only its explicit ID is admitted by the forward runner. Research branch IDs, arbitrary IDs, AI-generated policy identifiers and unrecognized start-request fields cannot alter thresholds or grant execution authority. A successful research ranking still does not promote any strategy automatically. The admitted experiment remains unvalidated and paper-only.

Unavailable/malformed observations and long observation gaps clear the entry history; recovery must accumulate fresh warmup observations. None/non-dict quotes, missing timestamps, stale observations and provider errors follow the same bounded failure pause. Tests exercise temporary outage recovery, long-gap warmup reset, three failures while holding, paused persisted state, explicit tier-off exit resume, session-counter transaction rollback, restart/stale evidence, immutable policy copies, unknown policy rejection, API bounds and session-cap admission.

No real-feed forward run was started for this batch. Next remains sustained public-data/outage evidence, independent strategy review and bounded broader scheduling. M7 remains partial; green injected-data tests do not close forward-operation or profitability gates.

## v0.60.0 — forward quote provenance and execution-evidence review

Each fetched forward observation is now recorded before it can cause a modeled fill. Evidence contains only allowlisted quote fields, evaluation/cycle/session identifiers, eligibility outcome, source classification and policy fingerprint. Standard reader observations are labeled single-source/unattested; supplied transports are labeled injected/unattested. Extra account/key fields are never retained. Missing or malformed quote outcomes are recorded as rejected evidence when storage is available.

Evidence uses a per-session local hash chain and transactional head/count record. Append checks the current head/tail; review checks all retained rows, order, identities, chain continuity and count/head agreement. There is a 100,000-observation global cap with no automatic deletion. Evidence write failure, cap exhaustion or head corruption pauses forward operation before a new order is submitted. Local hashes are not externally anchored and cannot prevent coordinated rewrites.

`GET /api/paper/forward/sessions/{id}/evidence` and PORTAL review summarize observation span, gaps, rejection counts, distinct/repeated exchange timestamps, source modes and local integrity. Read-only lookup retrieves both active and archived session orders from the execution journal. Modeled fills are compared with the corresponding recorded bid, ask and receipt timestamp; session BUY/SELL counts are checked against filled orders. Policy payload fingerprints are recomputed. Disagreements are reported, never silently repaired.

Review while a session is running is explicitly provisional: session evidence and accounting are separate database snapshots. No report grants release approval, verifies profitability or certifies independent feed truth. Pre-evidence legacy sessions correctly report missing quote evidence; historical observations are not reconstructed or invented.

The public ticker adapter now timestamps actual receipt after the default HTTP transport returns, while preserving deterministic caller-supplied times and separate exchange-time freshness checks. Tests cover delayed transport receipt, quote/fill agreement, injected-source labeling, payload edits, tail deletion, head corruption, evidence-cap admission, failed writes, extra-field exclusion, counter mismatch and review API boundaries. No sustained real-feed session was run for this batch.

Next: sustained real-source forward evidence, independent attestation/strategy review and broader scheduling/physical history offload. These remain acceptance work; M2/M6/M7 are not complete.

## v0.72.4 — exchange freshness at the durable fill boundary

Closed an exchange-time gap remaining after receipt-time refresh: a quote could pass its ten-second exchange-age check at intake but cross that boundary during write-lock/replay delay while its receipt remained less than two seconds old. The runner now carries exchange time into its post-lock runtime hook and checks age against the exact continuity-checked wall sample, removing a second unchecked wall-clock read. Both entries and exits fail closed, clear warmup and cancel unfilled orders; prior holdings remain authoritative. Retained-evidence crosschecks independently compare fill evaluation to timezone-aware source exchange timestamps and preserve disagreements in portable review blockers. Fixed policy parameters/fingerprint remain unchanged.

## v0.72.5 — fail-closed order cleanup and uncertain acknowledgments

Committed fills and session fill counters remain authoritative; no fill is invented or reversed. Retained evidence/export keeps an explicit cleanup-review blocker. Failed diagnostic persistence still leaves the runner stopped with interrupted-session/outstanding-order admission protection.

## v0.75 — received-asset fee and balance projection foundation

Added a bounded pure BTC/USD interval projection driven by explicit execution IDs/prices and execution-linked fee activities. It applies BTC buy fees to received quantity and USD sell fees to cash, keeping both fee units separate. Identical identities deduplicate without changing amounts; conflicting IDs fail, missing/unlinked fees and unknown coverage remain blockers. Explicit fill sums/averages are compared with order snapshots, timestamps are checked and supplied closing balances must match exactly. No prices/fills are derived from cumulative averages, no missing fee becomes zero, and no existing account is written.

Even consistent input always leaves broker-account reconciliation, routing and ledger application false because opening balances, activity links and completeness are caller supplied. The scope is aggregate interval balance conservation, not authenticated coverage or intraperiod risk permission. Added a repeatable CLI fixture, read-only fixture API and beginner-described PORTAL control. The fixture demonstrates 1 gross BTC bought, a 0.001 BTC fee, sale of only 0.999 BTC and a USD sell fee, with no real market prices or return claim. M8 remains open.

## v0.77 — retained Matrix history beyond the active sampling window

Added a retained-history table alongside the active Matrix observations. At the active cap, completed UTC days move in one FULL-synchronous transaction; all original IDs, timestamps and payloads remain. The latest active ID is preserved to prevent identity reuse after long downtime. Independent total admission caps retained observations at six million (about 416 active days at the current six-second UI cadence, not guaranteed continuous coverage). Source paper accounts and execution journals remain untouched. This is logical retention within a growing database, not physical offload, disk reclamation, encrypted storage or unlimited history.

Range reads combine both tables under one read transaction, rank only IDs and load at most 1,200 selected original JSON points. Downsampled graphs remain dots and do not claim to preserve every extremum. Existing databases remain readable without GET schema writes; the next observation adds retention tables. PORTAL now displays retained counts and total capacity. Headless browser fixtures exercise the capacity readout as well as Matrix modes.

## v0.78 — byte-aware Matrix capacity and failure-safe collection

Added independent observation database byte and free-space admission alongside row retention limits. The collector uses a 1 GiB SQLite page ceiling, 256 MiB free-space reserve plus rollback-journal/write headroom and a conservative temporary archive-copy estimate. Checks run before quote IO and again after fetch; post-fetch denial rolls back any pending archive move. New point payloads are bounded at 64 KiB. An actual SQLite page-allocation failure returns an unrecorded result with the prior database intact. Missing-volume path traversal fails explicitly rather than looping, disk-probe errors release the local guard, and connections close on all paths.

The read-only history report exposes database/disk headroom and warnings; PORTAL shows MiB capacity and practical collection messages without raw storage codes. Node and headless browser fixtures cover the added capacity display. These are local observation-only budgets: no execution stop, broker permission, automatic pruning, paid service, encryption or cloud deployment is introduced. Journal/backup/other data usage and external disk races remain distinct; byte limits may stop collection before the row-based approximate year capacity.

Tests cover read-only telemetry without creating the data directory, byte and disk denial before transport, temporary archive byte/disk budget denial, post-fetch disk loss with rollback/retry, actual SQLite allocation failure with quick_check, oversized sample rejection/retry, disk-probe failure cleanup and nonexistent-volume termination. Existing archival recovery and verified offline backup/restore tests remain in the suite.

Final v0.78 validation: 618 tests passed, including 11 new storage-admission/failure cases. Full Python compilation, PORTAL JavaScript syntax and both actual Node controllers passed. Preexisting databases above the byte budget are also blocked without truncation, even if they contain reusable pages. One existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning remains.

## v0.79 — collective hub foundation and trained multi-horizon momentum research

Added a bounded canonical public-data hub with compressed rows, cross-collector idempotency, numeric normalization, conflict quarantine and per-market/interval generations. Hash-addressed compressed dataset/model packs are read-only attributed, size/decompression checked, synced and atomically published; repeated unchanged preparation reuses objects rather than making collector copies. Active snapshot generation is rechecked when publishing a model, and any new data/conflict invalidates cached signals. Offline backup/restore preserves sealed models and deduplication. This is local infrastructure, not a shared cloud deployment, independent source attestation, encryption or unlimited storage.

Added three actual trained ridge momentum models at 1/3/12-bar horizons with short/medium/long momentum, volatility and volume features, training-only scaling, purged chronological holdout, zero-change/momentum comparison errors and nonzero-return directional scoring. Trained coefficients/scalers and dataset identity persist; unchanged datasets reuse the model. Small cached research signals read only the artifact and generation metadata, reject stale/future/changed data and remain separate from the forward runner. Five existing council fixture adapters share compact evidence and abstain; no actual external AI service or model execution authority was enabled.

PORTAL adds market, interval and window selectors with Build Hub & Learn, stored-data learning, cached prediction and status controls, all with practical hover help. Explicit builds make four bounded public history requests for up to 600 closed candles, directly deduplicate into the hub and prepare local research. The default transport rejects redirects/response URL changes and bounds timeout/body size. No network request was made in validation; injected responses exercised page filtering, retention/retry and duplicates. Builds are guarded against overlapping requests in this backend, and selection controls freeze during preparation. No account, credential or order data is uploaded or accepted into the hub.

The read-only roadmap adds the collective hub goal and records the learned-baseline/data components while keeping source, scientific strategy, cloud and release acceptance open. ## v0.82 — repeated-refit evidence, delayed-fill costs and less duplicated research work

Strengthened the local council before adding unattended refresh: existing tail-block error checks now have a separate three-fold expanding-window diagnostic. ## v0.83 — opt-in bounded local learning refresh

Added a local research refresh worker that requires explicit start and an active Portal, captures the selected market/interval and 240–1,000-bar window, and always starts stopped after restart. It checks already stored history once per minute, imports it once, coalesces unchanged generations, reuses current packs and retains the existing complete validation/council/cost evidence. It makes no public price, provider, credential or cloud requests. It does not replace a continuous market-data collector or change the forward runner's policy/order authority.

Sessions stop at 60 polls or eight training attempts. Attempts invalidated by concurrent data changes still count; idle/duplicate/busy work does not train. Manual/worker preparation shares one nonblocking backend job lock. A separate OS file lock excludes another refresh worker on the data directory, remains held during an in-flight stop and releases on process death. Failed candidates preserve prior model references/artifacts; old generations still abstain. Missing data waits within budget; faults pause with sanitized reasons. Nonfinite/regressing clocks, directory changes and lost Portal admission fence intake/publication. Existing disk/object/data quotas remain in force; no evidence is evicted.

Stop/Pause/Halt/reset/shutdown fence worker publication through its stop/publication lock. A calculation, import or candidate seal may finish after stop, without publishing a new current reference after acknowledgment. If publication acquired the guard first, stop acknowledgment waits for that publication to finish. Ownership/start admission remains denied until prior work/thread ends. Refresh shutdown runs even if another component's cleanup raises. This is not preemptible hard CPU-time enforcement, multi-host coordination or a durable worker audit journal.

Research adds Start/Stop/Status controls and captured-scope/budget/status readout, automatically refreshed by read-only local GET while that tab is visible. All selectors/buttons retain practical black/purple beginner help. To Do has an optional running-state observation; it cannot arm the worker or certify model quality. ## v0.84 — separately opted-in public history collection

Closed the source-to-learning gap with an optional public-history mode in the existing bounded refresh session. Saved-only remains the UI/API/core default. A separate unchecked checkbox plus explicit active-Portal start captures consent; selection, tab/status/To Do reads, restart and halt reset cannot enable collection. No paid model, account, broker, order or cloud authority is added.

Public mode bootstraps up to four sequential 150-bar windows, then requests one overlapping window only when a new UTC candle interval has closed with a five-second settling delay. Requests are charged before transport, including failures, under a 12-admission session cap alongside the existing poll/fit-attempt limits. Same-boundary polls do no network work; canonical rows deduplicate, conflicts quarantine and generation changes trigger the existing complete model/cost review. Missing or late source data is not fabricated and does not establish freshness/point-in-time provenance.

Preflight checks conservative row, database, conflict, artifact and disk headroom before each request; transactional admission still enforces actual writes and handles concurrent disk changes. The fixed reader rejects redirects/response URL changes before body access and bounds timeout and body size. Wall/monotonic continuity, stop, root and Portal admission are rechecked after the transport returns, before ingestion, and before model publication. A previously admitted request can finish after Stop, but its body cannot be ingested after acknowledgment. Stop ownership is held until the cycle completes. Partial failures retain earlier committed pages and old packs; network/clock/storage/conflict failures pause with sanitized reasons.

Research shows captured saved-only/public mode and request budget. Changing consent or scope requires stop/restart. Help explains optional external price access without suggesting paid AI or account connectivity. Browser tooling checks that toggling the checkbox alone sends no write or starts work. Model-note/To Do and roadmap/handoff documentation distinguish current runtime capability from historical notes in older sealed packs.

Thirteen new cases exercise default network silence, four-page bootstrap/closed-boundary coalescing/incremental retraining and deduplication, settling delay, stop-during-fetch response discard/ownership, charged partial source failures with retained pages/privacy, request budget, pre-network storage denial, receipt-time and in-fit wall-clock jumps, quarantined revisions preserving accepted history, exact response identity before body access, explicit/default API mode forwarding and bounded default response reads/timeouts. Actual Node controller checks both saved-only and explicit public-opt-in request bodies/readouts. All network data in tests is injected; no actual public feed request or production paper session was started.

## v0.85 — research engine, separate paper throttle and independent emergency cut

Implemented the owner’s engine/throttle contract. Start Portal starts the read-only public observer and bounded learning checks with captured Research selections: BTC-USD/hourly/1,000-bar maximum and public history selected by default. Explicit saved-only selection remains available. Startup is idempotent while on, never replenishes worker budgets on a retry, and rolls back to engine-off on expected startup failures. Checkbox selection, navigation, status, To Do and process startup do not arm work. Standalone legacy refresh API/core omitted flags remain saved-only; Portal and UI explicitly pass their selected public flag. Existing request/poll/fit/storage limits and source/ingestion/publication fencing remain.

Added global Engage Paper and Disengage controls with practical help. Engage confirms the fixed experimental BTC policy, requires the existing entry-tier/account gates and operates compatible virtual accounting only. Research council output remains advisory and never replaces the fingerprinted runtime policy. Deposited real funds and broker execution remain unsupported. Disengage stops the paper session while keeping research on; engine off stops both.

Emergency Stop now publishes a local trading latch immediately, persists durable halt/cancellation and fences runner cleanup, without stopping the observer, learning or account observation. Pending simulated remainders cancel; committed fills/holdings remain and are not liquidated. Reset clears the cut only, preserves the engine’s state and never engages. Serialized admission with post-start checks prevents slow Engage from surviving a cut or engine-off. A newer cut wins over a concurrently blocked durable reset. The emergency button stays available during pending Engage and after an unconfirmed cut. Manual ledger buy/sell paths recheck the cut epoch under trading admission after quote IO, so pre-cut requests cannot write after a cut even if subsequently reset. Generic simulated-intent admission is also serialized. Tier disengagement still permits ordinary risk-reducing exits when no emergency cut is latched.

Fourteen new cases cover engine defaults/idempotency/no trading, explicit saved-only scope/validation, real partial fill cancellation/reconciliation and retained holdings, reset/disengage independence, startup rollback, concurrent slow Engage vs cut/engine-off, reset vs newer cut, storage failure local latching, actual in-flight runner fetch fencing, engine-off reset, Matrix observation under a cut, actual UI controller behavior and buy/sell quote requests spanning cut+reset. Updated legacy API/controller/browser expectations, startup checklist, guides, handoff instructions and source-generated read-only roadmap. All controls retain black/purple beginner tooltips.

## v0.86 — explicit funding/autonomy release stages and efficient public quote intake

Expanded the source-controlled roadmap with nine product delivery stages alongside the existing nine technical evidence tracks. It now explicitly distinguishes usable research, model/strategy evidence, experimental autonomous paper, connected broker paper, secure provider-held deposits/spendable cash, governed live autonomy, secure computer-off/shared-hub operation, performance/resilience acceptance and the final tested 1.0 release. Source/API/generated ROADMAP and PORTAL show current progress, remaining work and dependencies. Funding remains preview-only, broker/live/cloud unimplemented or blocked; no stage grants money-movement permission. The agreed production workflows must pass their acceptance gates and owner approval before 1.0; a release never activates live money by itself. Updated VERSIONING, startup/handoff instructions and retained recovery references.

The next implemented efficiency stage coalesces concurrent runtime default public ticker calls by fixed product: one pending request per BTC/ETH/SOL market, independent markets, five-second follower waits and no lock held over HTTP. Existing GET-only URL/redirect/body/socket-timeout checks remain. A follower timeout cannot cancel the owner, create a replacement request or return old data. All leader exits, including unexpected failures, signal followers and release the pending slot; explicit later reads may retry. Injected transports neither use nor populate default cache/flights; explicit fixture clocks have a separate cache mode and do not join production flights or metrics.

Preserved the under-three-second cache window and original receipt/exchange timestamps. Added monotonic-age checks, wall/monotonic continuity before runtime transport publication and cache reuse, and each follower’s own fresh-at-return check. Returning a quote cannot relabel its age or extend exchange lifetime. Returned dictionaries are independent from retained/shared results, preventing a caller from mutating other readers’ quotes. Clock/source faults fail without stale fallback. The fixed policy, order admission, forward source labels, post-lock execution checks and emergency-cut semantics remain. Sharing one observation is not independent source confirmation.

Added no-store GET /api/public-data/efficiency and Research → Check Feed Efficiency. The endpoint reads copied process-local aggregate counters only, without source/storage/account/model/order IO. It reports reader calls, transport attempts/successes/failures, cache hits, shared waits/returns, bounded wait timeouts, active pending products and successful transport timing scope. No raw quote, error, key or user-entered data appears. No metrics reset/write API or durable state is added. Practical black/purple help, duplicate-click guard, failed-read cleanup and intercepted browser/controller coverage are retained. The same Node controller test also renders the newly explicit funding/final-release roadmap stages.

Twenty-six intake cases cover twelve concurrent readers with one transport, independently pending fixed markets, failed/unknown-exception leader cleanup, follower timeout without replacement/cancellation, wall/monotonic expiry and drift/regression, in-flight clock faults, stale follower return, original receipt/copy isolation, injected/fixture clock separation, no stale fallback, invalid clocks, exchange lifetime, zero-IO/no-store diagnostics, actual UI control and an actual paper runner cut while a research reader shares its pending fetch. Runner accounting remains halted/reconciled, with no fill and ownership retained until the fetch cycle ends; research still receives the quote. One additional roadmap case verifies explicit funding/live/final blockers, dependency ordering and immutable snapshots.

## v0.87 — reserved emergency control and queued-reset fencing

Moved emergency admission out of the ordinary synchronous request-worker pool. POST halt now latches process-local intake and advances the cut epoch on the async path, then submits or coalesces one cleanup Future on a lazy single dedicated worker. Neither saturated AnyIO feed workers nor the asyncio general executor consume that capacity. Repeated cuts do not add cleanup jobs. Durable gateway halt/cancellation, the runner’s existing stop fence and audit still finish before a successful acknowledgment. Failed submission retires the executor and cancels any abandoned item, including the case where submit enqueues work before thread start fails. Nothing starts at process boot or from status GET.

Shielded submitted cut work from cancellation of the awaiting HTTP task. A concurrent Future completion callback owns the pending/reset fence, independent of request lifetime or event loop. Disconnected callers do not cause premature reset permission or unobserved async exceptions. Lifespan shutdown drains this lane before other cleanup, rejects fresh queue admission while draining and preserves the local halt on rejected requests. The executor can be recreated for a later application lifespan without retaining running permission. Research/observer/learning remain independent of the trading cut and all existing durable/partial-fill/clock/ownership controls remain.

Fixed a reset race exposed by the overload/cancellation fixtures: checking pending only after acquiring trading control allowed a reset received during cleanup to wait and then clear the just-completed cut. Reset now rejects pending cuts and captures the epoch in async admission before general-worker scheduling, then validates again under trading control and after durable reset. A reset queued before a newer cut cannot clear it when the worker queue unblocks. The reserved cut worker is never used for reset. Reset still preserves research on/off state and never engages paper.

GET status is now an async/no-store/zero-IO route around the existing synchronous snapshot helper. It reports CLEAR, PENDING, VERIFIED or UNCONFIRMED. Verification follows successful local cut/stop/audit completion or validated durable halt recovery; a mere local request is not verification. PORTAL displays these states, disables Reset during pending cleanup and keeps Emergency Stop available. UI/backend statuses do not imply broker cancellation, liquidation, whole-account/strategy health or live-money authority. All practical black/purple help is retained. Browser fixtures now render all cut phases and disabled Reset help without posting stop/reset; the existing Node controller checks those phases too.

Thirteen new cases cover the actual ASGI control/status routes with blocked feed capacity and with both general pools blocked; twenty concurrent cuts sharing one job; pending reset rejection; successful/failed cleanup after caller cancellation; worker-construction and submit failures plus actual thread-start failure/abandoned-work cancellation and explicit retry; lifecycle drain/reopen; failed stop/audit confirmation; status without storage or worker allocation; and reset queued before a newer cut. Existing start/reset races, partial cancellation, in-flight quote, recovery, modeling and feed-sharing regressions remain.

## v0.88 — immediate runner cut signal, real storage contention and crash boundaries

Closed the gap between async local API latching and the runner’s execution checks while durable halt waits for storage. Emergency admission now sets a distinct runner Event before durable-worker/lane admission, without taking runner control/execution locks or doing IO. The runner observes it before preparation, before replacing a prior session identity, on cycle/fetch/submit boundaries and in the existing post-SQLite-write-lock fill clock hook. A missing cleanup worker or busy database cannot erase this local execution veto. Preparation, ordinary stop and engine retries never clear it; only the coordinator does so after durable reset and the final epoch check under cut admission. Reset keeps _stop set and never starts trading.

Kept the cut Event separate from _stop to preserve existing STOP diagnostic handling and ownership lifetime. A cut-only worker exit does not release ownership prematurely; fenced stop/cycle completion still owns release. enabled=false now also means local intake blocked and does not claim durable cleanup or all threads finished. Forward status exposes cut_requested and PORTAL shows LOCAL TRADING CUT REQUESTED. Beginner help explains the immediate signal plus pending/verified/unconfirmed semantics. Concurrent start conflicts caused by the cut return HTTP 409 rather than treating the interruption as invalid input. Policy fingerprints, accounting/fee assumptions, data freshness, source labels, linked-ledger rules and normal entry permissions remain unchanged.

Eleven new cases use actual SQLite write reservations on order and linked-portfolio files, actual BUY/SELL fill transactions waiting for the order write lock, a real busy timeout with unconfirmed response and explicit retry, missing worker admission, signal retention across prepare/stop/engine retry and a cut at the START diagnostic boundary. An interrupted recovery now preserves the previous session identity and retained history before a new START is admitted. The post-lock hook vetoes the waiting fill even when the storage lock is released, retaining prior holdings/partial fills, canceling remainders and keeping research on. Durable cancellation and the full stop fence still precede a success acknowledgment.

Two of those cases kill a real child before durable halt commit and after commit while audit/acknowledgment is withheld. Another fresh process checks authoritative truth, linked-account agreement, exact retained filled notional/quantity, pending versus canceled remainders, stopped startup and blocked admission/released OS ownership. Before-commit death loses the volatile signal: startup may report CLEAR, but neither engine nor paper resumes and an outstanding order blocks admission for review. After-commit death recovers the durable halt. No repair, inferred completion, deleted evidence or automatic liquidation is introduced. Persistent pre-commit intent remains explicit future work.

## v0.89 — independent local cut intent and conservative restart admission

Added a bounded secret-free presence latch beside the order database. The dedicated cut worker creates/flushes/fsyncs it before SQLite halt, under trading control; POSIX also syncs the directory. Existing/incomplete/malformed entries are retained conservatively without following contents. Startup combines presence with validated durable recovery, sets the runner cut signal and never repairs orders or resumes work. A pre-commit process death now recovers UNCONFIRMED with pending remainders preserved; a validated committed halt recovers VERIFIED. The two actual kill/fresh-process fixtures assert the intent existed at their boundary and retain exact filled quantity/notional and account reconciliation.

Explicit reset fences the runner, commits database reset, removes intent and performs the existing final epoch check. A newer cut wins and restores intent/halt; intent-clear failure leaves local admission blocked and UNCONFIRMED, even when database reset committed. Intent persistence failures still attempt durable halt and runner cleanup but cannot acknowledge full success. Read-only status remains zero IO. No order-policy, source, fee, accounting or live-money authority changed.

## v0.90 — durable disconnected broker request recovery and Windows cut-latch correction

Advanced M8's broker-paper foundation with a separate account-scoped durable request journal, without enabling an adapter or broker connection. Canonical BTC quantity/IOC-limit terms and client IDs are immutable; a committed uncertain-submission boundary survives restart without granting retry. The boundary records a future transport's uncertainty, not a send operation. Broker UUIDs and allowlisted cumulative snapshots replay through the existing identity/terms/time/quantity/state checks. Partial quantity survives pending/confirmed cancellation; cumulative average never becomes an inferred incremental execution price. Fees, holdings, cash, entitlement and account reconciliation remain unverified.

A bound account digest and append-only canonical hash chain are replayed on each access. Concurrent writes serialize with BEGIN IMMEDIATE and FULL synchronous commits. Observation validation and append share one transaction and one retained-prefix replay, avoiding redundant full-history rebuilds. Current duplicates add no event; conflicting IDs/terms, stale observations, changed broker IDs, reused broker UUIDs, modified/missing chain prefixes and capacity exhaustion fail without accepted state changes. Caps retain every accepted request/event without eviction: 128 requests, 4096 events, 4096 bytes/event, 64 MiB SQLite/file admission and existing 256 snapshots/request. Missing read-only state does not create a journal; foreign/empty/damaged state is not silently initialized. Hashes are local consistency, not encryption/attestation or proof against valid-tail deletion. SQLite hot-journal recovery and authenticated event completeness remain separate review requirements.

Added a disposable CLI and no-store fixture GET plus Accounts → Check Broker Request Recovery. The example reopens a committed unknown request, records partial/pending-cancel/canceled observations and recognizes current replay. It uses temporary storage only, with no application-account access, transport, credentials, retry or synthetic-ledger writes. The button includes black/purple beginner help, duplicate-click suppression, sanitized failure and actual controller/intercepted-browser coverage. Contract/catalogue, source-generated roadmap, startup and continuity documentation now expose the foundation while keeping connected paper/funding/live/cloud gates open. Official original-client-ID lookup documentation was rechecked; no authenticated lookup was performed.

Twenty-seven journal cases cover preparation/uncertainty, fresh objects and actual child-process deaths after attempt/observation commit, lost commit acknowledgment, rollback after inserted data, account isolation, concurrent duplicate admission, reused broker UUIDs, partial cancellation, immutable identities, old/conflicting transitions, caps, corrupt prefixes, read-only/no-init behavior, extra-field filtering, forged preview terms, disposable CLI/API, sanitized failures and the actual UI controller. Two additional intent cases reproduce Windows EACCES with and without an existing latch. Exact v0.89 safety run 39069884776/job 117505112241 failed one test (787 passed) because Windows raises PermissionError rather than FileExistsError for exclusive creation over a directory. The corrected implementation conservatively recognizes existing latch presence after either exception, still fails when presence cannot be established, and preserves the original directory/reset rejection. This fixes the platform behavior; v0.90 still needs its own Windows result.

## v0.91 — retained lookup pacing and conservative broker-response recovery

Extended the disconnected account-scoped broker request journal with explicit durable lookup begin/completion events and a pure supplied-response classifier. The hypothetical lookup is GET-only at the fixed paper Trading API original-client-ID path, remains unsendable and never invokes HTTP, credentials, timers, account mutation or order submission. Admission requires the original uncertain-submission boundary and a nonterminal request. Four total lookup attempts per retained request, one pending lookup/journal, shared cooldowns and a reserved completion event slot survive reopening. Ordinary writes cannot consume the result slot while a lookup is pending. Process death or lost begin acknowledgment leaves the charged ticket pending for review; no automatic expiry or budget rearm exists.

Lookup completion and any accepted cumulative observation commit atomically after the existing identity/terms/time/state/quantity checks. Exact completion replays append nothing and cannot clear a newer ticket. A lost FOUND commit acknowledgment reopens the retained partial quantity rather than assuming failure. Timeout, network error, 404 and 5xx preserve uncertainty; no result grants resubmission. Local exponential waits are retained. Rate-limit responses apply a shared Retry-After seconds/HTTP-date wait; absent headers use a local 60-second fallback, while unsupported/unsafe/over-one-day waits require review rather than being truncated. Authentication and unsupported/redirect responses retain an account-review block on new lookup admission. Raw headers, error bodies, location fields and credentials are not stored. Supplied wall time is checked against retained activity for regressions but is not trusted monotonic time; this is not provider pacing, streaming/cancel integration or distributed execution ownership.

Expanded the existing temporary broker request fixture with a separate disposable timeout/rate-limit journal and an attempts/wait readout in Accounts. Black/purple beginner help, duplicate-click protection and sanitized errors remain; actual Node controller and intercepted browser fixtures verify the extra text. No general lookup/write/connection API was added. Updated source-generated read-only roadmap, contract/catalogue, startup instructions and continuity references. M8/D4 remain open because authenticated broker transport, account/asset eligibility, execution/fees/cash/position reconciliation, pacing/outages and independent acceptance are still required.

Thirty-five new cases cover response classification, Retry-After seconds/date/default/unsupported/oversized inputs, persisted pending lookup/ordinary-write fences, unknown submission after timeout/404, shared cooldowns/authentication review, retained four-attempt budgets, success/partial-cancellation and current duplicate completion, old-ticket rejection and replay during a newer pending lookup, invalid identity rollback, completion capacity reservation at the journal cap, backward/invalid/huge clocks, concurrent admission charging one attempt, failed result insertion, actual process death with fresh-process review, successful FOUND commit with lost acknowledgment and raw-header privacy. Existing request/identity/account/crash tests still pass. New event kinds extend the local v1 journal; old v0.90 readers reject them rather than discarding evidence. No compatibility migration or automatic repair is supplied.

## v0.92 — retrospective fee-reserved USD/BTC funding-path audit

Added a pure chronological audit of supplied BTC/USD execution history. Aggregate ending balances alone can hide an earlier unfunded buy or a sale before acquisition. The new audit checks timestamp-group debits against pre-group funds and reserves every declared execution-linked received-asset fee at its execution, including later postings. It does not infer prices, fills, missing fees, holdings or an ordering among equal timestamps. Same-time receipts cannot be assumed to fund another same-group execution; all pre-funded debits can pass without choosing an order. UTC equivalents group deterministically; microsecond resolution collapses finer timestamps conservatively. Retained minima cover opening/group-end boundaries, not unknown intragroup lows.

Any incomplete/conflicting aggregate evidence prevents path evaluation. Nonterminal orders still require reservation review, and fees exceeding an execution's own received asset remain a blocker. Review samples are capped at 20 with the full violation count/reasons retained. Success is supplied-history consistency only: spendable funds, broker account reconciliation, routing and ledger application remain false. This retrospective use of later-known fees must never enter earlier strategy decisions, model features or causal profitability backtests. Actual account holds, available/settled cash, other orders, transfers/deposits/corrections, source coverage and governed contemporaneous fee reserves still require authenticated reconciliation.

Refactored the aggregate projection internally to share validated normalized records with the new audit in one bounded pass; its public reconcile signature/results and historical input digest remain. The existing fixed fee fixture adds a cash_path result, standalone CLI and Accounts readout with practical black/purple help. Added duplicate-click protection, unknown-path review fallback, sanitized failure and no-store responses; no new general reconciliation/connection/write endpoint exists. Actual Node controller and intercepted browser traversal exercise the updated fee button alongside the request/lookup fixture. Source-generated roadmap, contract/catalogue, startup and continuity notes retain scope and open funding/connection gates.

Twenty-four new cases cover a funded fee-reserved round trip, positive final totals hiding an earlier USD deficit or sell-before-buy BTC deficit, delayed fee reservation, overselling fee-reserved BTC repaired only by a later buy, equal-time funding ambiguity in both assets, fully prefunded simultaneous execution, timezone/input-order invariance, submicrosecond ambiguity, nonterminal reservations, incomplete fees/executions/balances, excessive received-asset fees, duplicates/conflicts/caps/privacy, bounded review samples, empty unverified paths, disposable CLI/API, one normalization pass and sanitized no-store failures. ## v0.93 — explicit interrupted-lookup review with journal-head fencing

Closed the disconnected journal's manual recovery dead end without assuming a missing broker result. pending_lookup provides copied read-only current client/ticket/request/head/attempt/time inspection, including a missing-journal no-init result. interrupt_lookup requires the exact current account/client/ticket/head, a strict explicit confirmation and nonregressing supplied time. SQLite write admission rechecks the current head/ticket; LOOKUP_INTERRUPTED retains that review and replay verifies its expected head equals the actual retained predecessor. Success acknowledges after FULL commit. No timer, expiry, automatic interruption, transport cancellation or order resubmission was added.

The interruption preserves the original uncertain-submission marker, prior partial observations, all consumed lookup attempts, existing review blocks/waits and adds the conservative attempt-based cooldown. Late completion from its old ticket cannot apply. A completion winning the race changes the head and rejects a stale review; interruption winning the race rejects the late completion. Exact historical interruption replay appends nothing and cannot clear a newer pending lookup. The reserved completion slot supports the review at event capacity, and four interruptions cannot rearm the four-attempt budget. Failed insertion leaves pending state intact; lost acknowledgment after commit reopens the retained event idempotently. Copy/restore never grants automatic resume or broker ownership.

Extended the existing temporary request recovery example with reopened pending-ticket inspection, explicit interruption and rejected late reply. Accounts displays the fixture result with practical black/purple help, retaining duplicate guards and sanitized errors. Actual Node/controller and intercepted browser fixtures cover the added text. No actual ticket/account review or generic interruption write HTTP endpoint exists. Source-generated read-only roadmap, contract/catalogue, startup and continuity documentation distinguish declared local review from physical process/transport shutdown, authenticated broker reconciliation and distributed execution ownership. Old v0.92 readers reject the new event kind rather than discarding evidence.

Twenty-three new cases cover no-init/read-only copied inspection; strict confirmation, wrong ticket/client/account/head and regressing time; retained submission uncertainty, partial quantity, cooldowns and budgets; stale review after completion; exact review replay during a newer pending lookup; two-writer completion/interruption races; result-slot admission; rollback after insert; commit with lost acknowledgment; corrupted/account-mismatched journals; actual process deaths before and after review commit with fresh-process inspection; offline backup/restore; and a checksum-consistent but semantically wrong review predecessor. The latter proves replay checks the review reference as well as byte hashes. Accepted history remains retained, with no inferred broker outcome or account/ledger repair.
