## v0.94 — bounded opt-in paper account inspection

Implemented a separately opt-in GET-only paper HTTPS inspection primitive and conservative account/available-quantity normalization. This advances the transport/account foundation beyond supplied arithmetic, while leaving runtime connection and trading authority disabled. No application route, CLI, Portal control, vault/environment retrieval, persistence or automatic caller was added. Every call requires literal paper-read confirmation and supplied credentials. Validation uses injected responses only; no real credentials or broker requests occurred.

The standard reader uses only the fixed paper host, four GETs (account/positions/open orders/account), ordinary TLS verification, no proxies or redirects, exact response URLs, bounded JSON/content checks and decimal-preserving numbers. It rejects concurrent calls without queueing, limits each request and total sampled elapsed time, checks wall/monotonic continuity and permission around IO, closes failures and emits fixed errors. It never submits/cancels orders, follows redirects, retries, schedules, writes a journal or gains a runtime adapter. Synchronous IO boundaries are not hard cancellation or distributed coordination.

The account UUID is bound to any supplied expected ID before further reads and compared across the bundle. Allowlisted account fields must remain stable; missing/unknown permissions, currency/statuses, pending transfers/accruals, unsupported holdings and any open orders require review. Observed cash is capped by nonmarginable buying power, excluding leveraged buying power. Long BTC requires explicit available quantity; duplicate or impossible quantities fail. Output excludes unrelated private fields. Matching sequential observations are not an atomic portfolio snapshot or authenticated acceptance. No spendable-funds, account-reconciliation, ledger or routing flag becomes true.

The source-controlled read-only roadmap/catalogue and continuation notes record the primitive and retain D4/M8 as open. Secure credential onboarding and read authorization, actual inspected-account acceptance, original-ID lookup/pacing, execution/activity/received-asset fees and cash/position/hold reconciliation remain prerequisites to monitored provider-paper routing. Funding, governed live autonomy and secure computer-off operation remain unimplemented acceptance gates; no milestone or profitability claim follows from injected checks.

## v0.95 — explicit paper credential onboarding and read-only account controls

Added a separate Windows Credential Manager paper pair store and four explicit Accounts actions: save/replace, local status, one-time paper inspection and removal. Saving starts no network request. Save and delete acknowledge after verified readback, with unconfirmed/lost acknowledgments retaining a local read veto. Local mutation epochs invalidate active reads before vault IO; rejected overlapping removal still supersedes an older save's ability to rearm. Reads run without holding mutation admission, check permission around network IO and compare the retained entry again before returning. Final readback catches external entry changes. This remains local volatile fencing, not physical network cancellation, provider key revocation, distributed ownership or secure erasure of Python memory.

Registered four fixed same-origin loopback POST actions with bounded duplicate-free JSON, exact field sets, no secret-bearing model validation errors, fixed private failures and no-store responses. Blocking work is outside the event loop and the emergency cleanup lane remains independent. No GET read, arbitrary URL/transport, plaintext/env fallback, credential export or automatic request was added. Startup/global status/To Do do not open the paper vault, including when a pair is already saved.

The Accounts interface clears password fields before save requests, separately confirms storage/read/removal, prevents duplicate ordinary actions, permits removal during reads and suppresses obsolete replies. ## v0.96 — journal-bound original-ID GET recovery with post-lock fencing

Connected the existing bounded request journal and saved-key foundation to an internal explicitly confirmed paper recovery coordinator. It accepts only an existing uncertain original request and matching expected account UUID. Read-only original-term inspection creates no state. Lookup admission commits before any provider request, retains the four-attempt/event/cooldown/pending fences and never prepares, sends, cancels or retries an order. No new interface, CLI, application route, default path, background worker or automatic invocation was added; current Accounts inspection still reads only account/positions/open orders.

Extracted the account reader into a shared private GET session while preserving its contract/tests: fixed paper host/allowlisted paths, no proxies or redirects, exact URLs, bounded decimal-preserving JSON and time/permission checks. Added shared nonblocking inspection/recovery admission, request caps and one-use lifetime. Recovery checks account identity, fetches the original client ID and, for FOUND only, checks stable account fields again before validating and retaining the original normalized order. Non-found statuses/error enums credit no snapshot and make no extra request after rate/auth/timeout. No raw error body/header or credential is stored. Timeout/404 retains uncertainty and any partial fill; malformed or disagreeing success leaves the ticket pending for explicit review.

Checked journal begin/completion hooks sample session permission/time after real SQLite write admission/full replay and before commit. The HTTP outcome classifier uses that fresh admission time, preserving conservative cooldowns after storage waits. Callbacks never enter retained events; legacy explicit-time methods/schema remain compatible. Revocation before commit rolls back; a post-commit cut can suppress acknowledgment while preserving accepted history. Saved-key entry checks bracket completion, with cheap local mutation epochs at IO/write boundaries. These are local sampled checks, not physical HTTP cancellation or cross-process ownership. Legacy diagnostic journal records do not independently attest transport provenance or a broker.

Added integrated injected cases for successful original-ID partial observations, no-network preflight denials, schema/identity/account/term/status disagreements, bounded malformed success, HTTP/timeout/rate/auth classifications, duplicate/invalid Retry-After, preserved partials on 404, immutable copied terms, shared session admission and four-attempt restart limits. Fault cases include key removal/external entry changes, post-lock time sampling, post-insert rollback, post-commit acknowledgment loss, actual SQLite contention at begin/finish and process death before/after result commit with fresh-process prefix validation. Every provider/credential source is injected, with disposable journals only.

## v0.101 — combined retained-activity integrity review

Implemented internal read-only `BrokerActivityStore.review()` / `broker_activity_review.review` over one complete replay-validated snapshot. Group and deduplicate retained reported order snapshots once, preserve nanosecond timing, and select latest reported time for diagnostics rather than receipt/arrival order. Equal-time conflicts anywhere in retained history cannot be silently resolved. Filled-quantity regression, terminal status/quantity/average revision and average changes without added quantity block merged normalization without deleting or repairing evidence. Global review admission is independently capped at 1,000 distinct orders; no partial account is returned above the cap.

Parser failure exposes an explicit unavailable/disagreement state with no partial financial normalization or raw private error.

Overlap, touching intervals and empty batches cannot prove history completeness. All reports remain REVIEW_REQUIRED and all coverage/account/funds/ledger/routing/live/resume flags false. No API/UI, automatic collection, credential access, order adapter, funding or cloud action was added; existing storage schema is unchanged.

## v0.101.1 — Windows passive-vault regression correction and illustrated roadmap

The v0.100 Windows safety workflow failed in test_real_http_process_starts_stopped_without_data_credentials_or_external_io: its denied-access counter observed an operation that Linux had not. Inspected run39076190982/job117523726045 logs (1 failed/1,312 passed). Reproduced the concrete cause using an injected Windows-only policy branch on Linux with keyring denied: provider_research.status called enabled twice, and each tried credential_vault.retrieve, attempting the OS store twice. Readiness/To Do call this passive status function. No actual credential or provider access was used during diagnosis. This violates the documented passive/no-vault contract and is a release blocker, not a reason to weaken the trial.

Corrected passive AI status to read a nonblocking process-local credential hint only. Explicit install/removal/retrieval/configured actions update the hint; current credential state/provider connectivity remain unverified. Restart or failed observation is NOT_CHECKED, not proved absence. Actual research requests still re-read credentials and cannot use a cached positive hint as authority. Passive status does not wait behind the OS-operation lock. Added unknown/stale labels and practical existing-control help in Portal and corrected readiness/To Do descriptions. Reviewed public-data requests remain separately confirmed; optional saved keys can be checked at actual explicit request time without a passive startup vault read. The Alpaca paper credential entry/explicit status flow is unchanged.

Strengthened the real HTTP release profile to exercise readiness/provider status/To Do and inject the Windows-only AI policy gate on every host while all keyring operations remain denied. The report labels this as INJECTED_WINDOWS_ONLY_GATE, not native Windows evidence. Added 10 passive Windows-policy/cache/actual-request/OS-lock tests plus an actual AI-status Node-controller regression, bringing controller coverage to ten. Updated intercepted browser fixtures/readout assertions for NOT_CHECKED status; no local rendered-browser pass is claimed.

Added a source-controlled visual progress overview to generated ROADMAP.md and read-only /api/roadmap: local research/experimental paper foundation → current broker-paper integration → sustained/independent validation → funding/governed live and computer-off cloud → complete acceptance/owner approval → 1.0. Keep major product gates visible; iteration numbers and test counts cannot be translated into percent complete or an automatic release.

Final local validation: **1,351 full regression tests passed**, all ten actual Node controllers passed, current full Portal JavaScript syntax and nexus/tests/tools compilation passed, pip check found no broken requirements. One existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning remains. The refreshed six-profile source-fingerprinted trial report is saved as validation/release_trials_0_101_1.json after final verification. Native Windows regression/browser/packaging results for this corrected source remain required; predecessor v0.100/0.101 results must not be represented as acceptance of the fix. Exact v0.99 remains the latest fully verified Windows packaging/browser checkpoint at this review.

Next priority is verifying the corrected exact source on Windows, then authenticated broker-paper coverage/pacing/fee/cash/position/hold/order-lifecycle acceptance and sustained real-feed operation. ## v0.101.2 — optional AI credential boundary and persistence checks

Release audit found two concrete weaknesses in the older optional-AI credential flow: framework validation could echo invalid secret inputs before the local-origin handler ran, and the vault wrapper returned the global keyring dispatcher after checking a backend, without verifying save/removal readback. This batch brings that flow closer to the already bounded paper-account controls; it does not connect an AI provider or broker or close independent security acceptance.

Replaced secret-bearing Pydantic request handling with `ai_onboarding` on the existing POST connect/disconnect URLs. Local peer/Host/exact Origin is checked before bounded duplicate-free JSON parsing; connect accepts exactly api_key, disconnect preserves legacy empty-body support plus JSON {}. Unexpected fields/types, oversized/duplicate/malformed input and invalid secret characters return fixed no-store errors without input echoes. Explicit OS actions run through to_thread; passive AI status remains vault-silent/nonblocking and now also returns no-store. Backend exceptions never cross the endpoint.

The AI vault now returns the exact checked built-in WinVaultKeyring instance, rejects similarly named/fallback/subclass backends, validates bounded printable ASCII secret records, verifies save via readback and removal via confirmed absence, and treats an already absent entry idempotently. Corrupt stored values cannot enable research. Lost save/removal acknowledgments leave an unknown hint and cannot claim rollback or absence; only explicit fresh checks/retries resolve them. No automatic rollback/delete/retry occurs. UI help and confirmation clarify that local deletion does not revoke the provider key, change environment configuration or cancel already in-flight work. The separate paper entry and actual request-time credential checks remain intact.

Added 39 injected-storage/local-ASGI cases covering origin-before-parse, remote peers, malformed/oversized/duplicate/body/type/control-character input, fixed privacy/no-store failures, exact backend binding and no second global dispatch, matching save/delete readback, idempotent absence, malformed stored keys, POST-only controls, unconfirmed/lost acknowledgments and event-loop/status responsiveness during an injected slow OS save. Existing passive-status read-count expectations now include the explicitly authorized save verification read.

Validation is recorded after final regression and fresh six-profile source-fingerprinted trials below. OS stores and all provider transports remain injected; no actual credential, AI/broker request, funds movement or cloud resource was accessed. Native Windows profile/ACL/credential lifecycle and independent security review remain open. At CI inspection, v0.101.1 safety run39076946142/job117525949733 passed its complete Windows regression step and was running bounded trials; packaging run39076946113/job117526005603 remained in Install and test. The earlier v0.101 safety/packaging failures are historical and are not new-source acceptance. Exact v0.99 remains the fully inspected packaging/browser checkpoint until newer workflows finish.

Next: complete corrected-source Windows acceptance, then durable shared broker pacing and authenticated activity/fee/cash/position/hold/order-lifecycle reconciliation before provider-paper routing. Continue sustained forward/source/model/security/risk validation and governed funding/live/cloud implementation. Keep source/generated roadmap, acceptance evidence and explicit owner release/live authorization gates intact.

Added paper_read_pacing.PaperReadPacing with explicit path/account only. The account inspector, original-ID recovery, activity collector and saved-key wrappers optionally share it. It commits a whole <=four-GET session reservation before IO, coordinates cooperating processes through SQLite, retains success/rate/auth/transport outcomes and enforces a conservative 60-second minimum from recorded result time with bounded Retry-After extension. Exact account/type checks, canonical/hash replay, FULL atomic commits, post-lock/precommit admission clocks, fixed 256-session/512-event plus binding and 4 MiB database limits, reserved completion capacity and immutable retained history are enforced. No API/UI/default path/timer/retry/automatic block clearing, provider quota claim or new order authority exists; default manual Portal inspection is unchanged.

Faults, malformed/changed account data, clock/permission revocation, process death and lost acknowledgments can leave a pending reservation indefinitely. Authentication/unsupported outcomes require review. No automatic expiry, rollback inference, history deletion or journal switching is a supported recovery. Account/head/ownership-fenced explicit operator recovery and actual quota/account acceptance remain necessary before routine runtime use. Pacing, original-request and activity journals are separate transactions; a committed pacing result does not certify successful caller delivery or final saved-key verification. This controls only callers sharing this exact local journal, not another program/machine/default caller or an entire broker identity.

Added 44 focused pacing cases, including injected status/timeout/date/duplicate-header outcomes, sharing all three readers/saved wrappers, no IO before reservation, post-lock clock sampling during real SQLite contention, competing processes, process death before BEGIN/RESULT commit and after committed BEGIN, lost BEGIN/RESULT acknowledgments, cap/foreign/corrupt/symlink/empty histories and lock release. ## v0.103.0 — OS-owned paper-read interruption review

Continued from verified branch head baa86cd70d13d076d53d12a0c637d5a99ab41a0b (v0.102.0). Added nonblocking OS ownership for every optionally paced _ReadSession, acquired before durable BEGIN and retained through all HTTP/result/context cleanup, including faults and lost acknowledgments. New BEGIN records hash the canonical journal path and original lock-file device/inode; checkpoints validate the held file. Reject missing/replaced/symlink/nonregular/oversized/hardlinked lock identities and hardlinked databases. OS close/death releases ownership, never a timeout. This coordinates cooperating local callers only; it is not distributed/broker-wide ownership or protection from an administrator rewriting history.

Added internal PaperReadPacing.interrupt with literal confirmation, explicit expected account/current head/pending token, matching retained owned-BEGIN identity and acquired same-file OS lock. A still-active reader, stale review, changed account/token/clock/permission, legacy unowned BEGIN or moved/copied/replaced ownership scope is rejected without clearing history. Append INTERRUPTED_UNKNOWN and preserve session counts plus at least 86,400 seconds of cooldown from review evaluation; no inferred absent/cancelled/successful request. Duplicate acknowledgment is idempotent only while this exact interruption remains latest and cannot change the original cooldown. Completed auth/review blocks remain blocked, limits are not reset, old results cannot overwrite the review, no order lookup ticket/accounting changes and nothing retries/resumes automatically. There is no new API/UI/default/scheduled invocation; default manual Portal inspection still omits optional pacing. Older v0.102 readers reject additive owned-BEGIN/INTERRUPTED shapes; legacy histories remain readable but their unowned pending reads cannot be cleared by this operation.

Added 34 interruption/ownership cases: active injected HTTP, actual separate-process owner/death behavior, real SQLite contention/post-lock clock sampling, explicit confirmation/account/head/token/clock/precommit failure, auth/rate blocks, replay-after-new-event rejection, process death before interruption commit and lost committed acknowledgment, completion-slot capacity, late results, wrong/closed owner, lock replacement/copy/hardlink/symlink cases, ownership retention during result acknowledgment loss and unchanged uncertain original-order recovery history. Extended the seventh fixed release profile to replay explicit owned interruption and its full-day cooldown while preserving the existing independent auth/legacy-pending fixtures.

## v0.118 managed paper-read pacing in Accounts

Accounts now provides **Read Paper with Pacing** and **Review Read Pacing**. Enter the expected canonical paper account UUID. The first action asks for separate consent before reading the saved pair and running the existing fixed-host four-GET inspector. It retains at least60 seconds of cooldown after success; pending/authentication/cap/interruption conditions require their existing waits or review. The second action replays local pacing only: no credential read, provider request, admission permission or missing-file initialization. New controls have practical beginner help and share duplicate-click, removal-during-read, stale-response and export invalidation safeguards. No startup, hover, tab or passive checklist action invokes them.

On Windows the managed journal is `%LOCALAPPDATA%/NexusOperative/paper-read-pacing/<account-sha256>.sqlite3` (home fallback when LOCALAPPDATA is absent). Source-only non-Windows fixtures use `~/.nexus-operative/paper-read-pacing/`. The account digest is an identifier, not encryption or anonymity. Browser requests cannot supply a path/transport/clock or provider URL; known source-checkout destinations are rejected. Only the same managed local journal coordinates cooperating callers. Existing **Read Paper Account Once**, explicit CLI journals, other profiles and other computers are outside its pacing scope. Never use these alternatives to bypass retained cooldown or uncertainty. This is not broker-wide quota/distributed ownership.

Removing saved keys does not delete or reset the journal. Failed reads may already have contacted the provider or retained success/pending evidence. Preserve history and review it; there is no automatic retry, pending expiry, reset, deletion or order activation. Diagnostic head hashes can equal a pending BEGIN digest; they are consistency references, not credentials or authority. Reports omit named pending-ticket fields and paths and keep all existing funds/order/source/profile/release flags unverified. Portable downloads remain inventory-only and exclude pacing/open-order terms. The journal is private plaintext outside the portable storage backup bundle; retain it separately under OS disk/ACL protection and never publish it. No new encryption/backup guarantee is made.

Development checks inject vault/HTTP responses and use temporary journals. Actual Windows credential/profile/ACL, authenticated account/fee/hold, sustained real-market, funding/live/cloud and independent release acceptance remain open. New exact-source Windows/browser/security CI is required; local controller/API tests are not native acceptance.

## v0.120 private journal file preflight

A disposable reproduction showed that opening a WAL-mode pacing database for status could create new -wal/-shm files. Pacing, supplied broker-activity retention and disconnected broker-request journals now share a bounded file preflight before SQLite opens. Reject nonregular files, leaf symlinks, multiple hard links, oversized files, sampled path/descriptor identity changes, unsupported SQLite read/write-version header bytes and any existing WAL/SHM sidecar, including an orphan beside a missing main file. Read at most20 unbuffered header bytes; recheck newly created files before SQLite initialization. Existing file caps, schema/account/hash replay, atomic DELETE-mode writes and rollback-journal recovery are retained. Existing invalid/empty databases still require ordinary schema/binding review; no guard initializes or repairs them.

Unsupported layouts fail for both reads and writes without converting journal mode, deleting sidecars or silently creating a replacement main database. Keep the entire private file set intact while stopped and seek a reviewed recovery plan; do not erase or force-convert files to bypass a block. These sampled local checks are not an atomic SQLite descriptor binding, protection against every concurrent path replacement, an ACL audit, encryption or source/financial attestation. Other application stores are outside this specific change; no universal filesystem guarantee is claimed.

The existing pacing release profile now requires actual WAL read/write denials and unchanged files. New cross-store tests reject FIFO/directory/symlink/hardlink paths before SQLite, retain orphan sidecars, cover descriptor replacement/growth and preserve normal DELETE-mode replay/duplicate behavior. Accounts pacing-status failures now correctly describe local-history review and explicitly say the check reads no keys and sends no broker request; preserve its files rather than changing credential setup automatically. No new runtime operation, retry, reset, broker routing or authority is added. Generate the current-version report before the full suite; all14 profiles and saved-evidence checks remain required. Actual provider/profile/account/fee/hold, sustained market, funding/live/cloud and independent/owner release gates stay open.

## v0.131 declared collection-window barrier

The private accepted-reconciliation composition now validates that the union of **accepted caller-declared creation windows** covers the entire envelope from the opening observation's first read to the closing observation's last read, before any balance arithmetic. Earlier synthetic reconciliation fixtures used observation dates outside their retained requested windows; they now use dates within those windows and explicitly prove the old out-of-window case is rejected. Only receipts referenced by replayed acceptance records contribute. Missing/changed receipts, a changed retained head or scope count, reversed/overlapping observation windows, outside endpoints and interior gaps fail closed. Merge adjacent/overlapping/duplicate intervals without bridging a positive gap; use canonical UTC nanoseconds, never float rounding. Bind the window-union digest in the v2 reconciliation input scope and retain the final three-journal recheck.

This is geometric coverage of requested filters, **not received activity coverage**. Exact endpoint equality does not verify a provider's inclusive/exclusive filter semantics; `requested_window_boundaries_verified`, source/activity/fee/hold/account/funds/routing/live/release flags remain false. Short final pages still only establish bounded cursor completion. Late/corrected executions, fees and sequential balance uncertainty remain open independent reviews. No provider/vault call, API/UI/control, automatic retry/continuation, evidence write or ledger/order/funding/cloud authority was added. Keep all actual observations, accepted journals and private diagnostic output outside Git; the existing standalone-report artifact guard applies to the v2 policy too.

The sixteenth release profile now requires out-of-window rejection, covered accepted geometry and false provider-boundary authority in addition to all prior acceptance/replay fences. Generate the current-version16-profile report before full tests, then verify saved evidence against the exact source. Regression checks include pre-arithmetic rejection with unchanged files, contiguous/overlapping/duplicate union, a one-second interior gap, exact endpoints and a one-nanosecond overrun. The general supplied observation-review tool retains its explicitly conditional diagnostic scope; this stricter barrier belongs to accepted reconciliation.

## v0.132 retained quote eligibility replay for sustained-trial review

The private forward-trial review previously counted adjacent eligible flags without independently checking the retained quote fields. It now replays a fresh session-local single-source FeedGuard plus exact product, explicit read-only/order-denied permissions, positive finite prices, spread/jump heuristics, recorded error absence, receive/exchange freshness and exchange regression checks at each recorded evaluation time. Require the exact currently admitted policy as well as its fingerprint; a self-consistent rewrite cannot install looser limits. Never use the reviewer's current time or upgrade a recorded rejected quote. This is a conservative check on supplied retained fields, not a replay of every runtime/ownership/accounting state or independent market-source verification.

Only a recorded eligible quote that passes revalidation and carries the existing public-reader label contributes to clipped same-session coverage intervals. Bad quotes break intervals. Keep original declared eligible/rejected counts with an explicit recorded-flag scope, add revalidated counts and count claimed-eligible contradictions separately. Any contradiction in the selected window blocks the proposed72-hour/95% target, even if geometric coverage still exceeds95%. All source, realism, broker, strategy, funds, live, automatic-start and release acceptance flags remain false. Preserve unknown/injected source exclusion, bounded read-only SQLite replay, no gap/session bridging and private-output handling.

Regression fixtures now supply complete synthetic quote fields; the old provider-only shape is explicitly rejected for coverage. Tests cover incomplete fields, stale/future receipt and exchange time, missing timezone, exchange regression, product/provider/permission mismatches, crossed/excessive spreads, nonfinite/overflow numeric data, price jumps, recorded errors, false flags, rehashed unadmitted policies and a nearly complete72-hour history containing one contradictory eligibility claim. The existing mandatory forward profile requires applied revalidation, consistent counts and zero contradictions in its injected runner fixture; it still cannot claim public coverage or market acceptance. No UI/API/control, actual provider/vault call, trading policy or execution authority changed. Run all16 exact-source profiles and saved-evidence verification before full regression and publication.


v0.132 Matrix observation standard (owner clarification): Singularity is one whole-account movement graph. Fractal is a coordinated multi-point view of all observed holdings, with individually traceable position histories. The implemented shared-time constellation encodes signed unrealized USD P/L around a zero ring on a shared range scale, keeps measured history below and removes missing/closed latest nodes. Angles and connecting edges are layout, never correlation, forecast or execution activity. ## v0.133 minimal first-run setup

Standing owner requirement: minimize setup to essentials and provide ready-made local paper defaults, editable afterward. No engine, order, account, feed, vault, provider or paid AI operation starts from this action. Existing traded/incompatible accounting still requires review, never automatic reset/migration. Restart remains stopped and the emergency throttle cut remains independent. The preset is a starting configuration, not statistically optimal settings or profitability evidence.

The budget check validates effective built-in defaults plus saved overrides without writes; malformed saved state remains unknown or needs review. Missing virtual accounting is marked When needed, not a mandatory setup form. Checks remain read-only and are observations rather than authority. The mandatory real loopback HTTP release profile still proves passive startup creates zero files, then exercises the normal settings GET/permissions/checklist path: only the settings database is created, Terrestrial is selected, each purpose is present, optional connections are not required, an explicit OFF survives reload and trading stays stopped. ## v0.142 broker handoff admission and recovery headroom

The disconnected request journal now offers `begin_submission_checked`: a new marker requires the exact observed journal head and original payload hash, then runs the caller-defined evaluated-time/admission callback after acquiring the SQLite write transaction and again before commit. Reject changed terms/head, invalid/regressing returned clocks, revoked/raised admission and already-marked attempts. Only a returned commit can acknowledge the marker. Lost return/commit uncertainty never creates resend permission: reopen and recover using the original client ID. Legacy idempotent `mark_submission_uncertain` remains a diagnostic helper and must never be used as authorization to invoke a transport again. No new event schema, request payload, runtime API, broker connection, credential access, order send or financial authority is introduced. The callback is caller-defined, not an independent account/risk/funds approval; any future adapter must separately recheck authorization immediately before actual IO after marker acknowledgment.

Count two event slots per unresolved/nonterminal attempt for a next original-ID lookup begin/result pair, or one when its lookup is pending. New prepare/attempt/nonterminal observation writes must leave that aggregate headroom. Lookup admission also preserves other unresolved requests' reserves. Completion/interruption must still record their outcome using the pending slot; a timeout/nonterminal outcome can leave a future reserve deficit rather than guaranteeing indefinitely many retries. Read-only recovery reports physical slots, reserved recovery slots, ordinary slots and any deficit. Terminal observations that release their own reserve remain admissible when a physical slot exists, including older deficit histories; full journals still cannot append. Do not delete history, invent terminal states or raise caps silently.

These are bounded cooperating-version event headroom checks under SQLite serialization, not byte/disk capacity reservations, distributed ownership, provider quota/funds guarantees or broker acknowledgment/fees/position reconciliation. Old/other writers and direct database edits are outside the reserve contract. Historical replay stays readable even when its available headroom is deficient. Existing lookup-pending fences, token/account binding, cooldowns, explicit interruption and no-auto-retry remain.

Tests cover stale heads/terms, pre/post-write revocation and regressing clocks with unchanged files, real SQLite contention, concurrent same-head begins, lost return after commit, separate reservations for multiple requests, protected next-lookup completion and terminal deficit cleanup. The existing pacing release profile now requires a disposable checked-marker/reopen/original-ID lookup/terminal-reserve-release composition with all transport/routing/account/financial/release flags false. Generate and verify all16 current-source profiles before the full suite. Actual broker-paper integration, authorized account/source/fee/hold reconciliation and independent/owner1.0 gates remain open.

A retained account-level lookup review/authentication block also rejects a new checked attempt for a different prepared request before invoking its caller admission callback. The mandatory fixture requires unchanged files for that denial; no credential reset or automatic unblocking occurs.

## v0.143 occasional percentage estimates and broker cooldown admission

Standing user preference: give occasional percentage milestone updates toward the full agreed1.0. Use the bundled `progress_estimate` baseline, not the version number or passing test count. Initial2026-10-18 assessment against b2a9bc6da241bb8c7f0e1dfe1b2ed6b251bf467c: roughly40% implementation, broad30–45% judgment range, low confidence. Nine relative-scope weights sum to100; implementation inputs are D1:85, D2:55, D3:70, D4:35, D5:0, D6:10, D7:10, D8:35, D9:0, producing weighted index38 before rounding to40. These are human engineering assessments, not measured tasks/hours, a statistical confidence interval, expected profitability or a release date. Foundations can count toward unfinished integrations; live/cloud operation is not deployed. No major stage has all acceptance requirements complete:0/9 fully accepted.

Reassess after substantial integration, acceptance evidence or scope changes; explain revisions and allow decreases. Small hardening patches need not move the estimate. Keep release/trading/funds flags false regardless of percentage. Persist changes in source and regenerate ROADMAP.md; use fresh independent snapshot copies. The existing hourly sprint prompt also now requests occasional clearly scoped percentage updates without changing its schedule or permissions.

## v0.144 durable shared-local Found Markets request budget

The optional public discovery worker now commits each request allowance before transport in a bounded local SQLite journal shared by cooperating Nexus processes on the same data root. Concurrent first use is serialized, only timestamps are retained, expiry is transactional, and stop/start or process restart cannot reset the rolling36-per-hour limit. A transport failure or lost return still consumes the committed allowance. Clock rollback and unsafe, malformed, oversized or unexpected WAL layouts refuse without public IO. Missing-path inspection, Portal GETs and application startup remain passive and create no journal.

This closes the earlier process-local reset gap only for cooperating Nexus processes sharing one `NEXUS_PORTABLE_DATA`. It does not coordinate other programs, machines or data roots on the provider's per-IP quota, attest a source, retain market evidence, establish profitable signals or grant any execution authority. Sustained real-feed/outage/shared-IP acceptance remains open. The weighted implementation estimate stays about40% with a30–45% low-confidence range and0/9 fully accepted stages because this hardening does not complete one of the nine major integration/acceptance stages.

## v0.145 passive broker handoff review and checked lookup clocks

`BrokerRequestJournal.submission_review(client_order_id, evaluated_at=...)` is an internal/source-level read-only diagnostic for the caller-supplied journal/account, not a new public path-selection API. Missing history leaves directories absent and reports REQUEST_NOT_PREPARED. Existing history uses bounded read-only replay and refuses account mismatch, corrupt/foreign/unsafe journals without repair. It returns the exact observed head/original payload hash, retained lookup clock floor/rate deadline, wait and capacity diagnostics with preparation, original-ID recovery, account review, pending lookup, clock, cooldown and reserve blockers. A clear result is LOCAL_MARKER_CHECKS_CLEAR only: no permission/risk/funds callback, event/byte/disk reservation, order or provider approval. All transport/trading/funding/release flags remain false. Re-evaluate the current head/terms and caller admission through begin_submission_checked; a stale clear review cannot bypass its lock-time checks. No schema/event/payload/cap changes or automatic retry/deletion occurs.

Checked original-ID lookup begin/result now reject evaluated-time regression between the pre-lock sample and locked admission, then again between the inserted event time and final callback before commit. Invalid/revoked/regressing final admission rolls back the actual inserted event; failed begin consumes no attempt, failed result preserves its pending ticket for explicit recovery. Equal times remain legal. Legacy supplied-time diagnostic methods and idempotent already-retained result acknowledgments retain their semantics; they do not create a fresh lookup/transport attempt. These checks remain local, not provider-wide quota or clock attestation.

The existing explicit Accounts offline request-recovery fixture now demonstrates a passive clear review followed by marked-attempt original-ID recovery. PORTAL rejects promoted preflight authority and clears the result on failure; missing legacy preflight evidence is unavailable. Practical button help and intercepted browser fixture cover the added explanation with the existing black/purple bottom-right help. No credentials, account storage, broker or live network is accessed by these fixtures. The mandatory pacing trial requires immutable passive review and actual post-insert clock rollback for both lookup begin/result. Review snapshots are not accepted account/funds/strategy/market evidence.

Rough progress remains about40% implementation, judgment range30–45%, low confidence,0/9 fully accepted stages. This coherent hardening batch does not close broker-paper, sustained market, native-profile, funding/live/cloud or independent/owner1.0 acceptance. Reassess estimates only on material evidence or scope change.

## v0.146 original request to retained explicit-fill review

`nexus.broker_request_activity_review.review` composes an exact BrokerRequestJournal and BrokerActivityStore for the same declared account under caller-supplied expected request/activity heads. It uses bounded read-only full replay, matches each observed original broker/client/side/status/quantity/average/time snapshot, and checks explicit REST fills against the immutable request's side/product, total/cumulative/leaves quantities and individual limit price. It reuses merged activity ambiguity, cumulative-chain, timestamp, fill-total and average-disagreement checks. All retained activity orders outside the request journal remain explicit review blockers; no match by client ID alone replaces a missing original broker observation. No execution price is inferred from cumulative averages. The report lists local agreement separately from status REVIEW_REQUIRED and persistent source/fee/hold/account blockers.

A good average cannot hide one bad execution: two unit fills at85 and113 average99, within a BUY100 aggregate limit, but the113 fill still violates that original limit. The analogous SELL98 example also requires review. Even exact local agreement never confirms complete activities, authentic provider data, fee links/timing, holds, balances, spendable funds or order authority. All source/coverage/reconciliation/ledger/routing/broker/live/funds/retry/release flags remain false. Review reads only the supplied journals; it writes no journal or ledger and accesses no credentials, network or default data path. Returned identity details are private plaintext; keep actual reports/journals outside Git. The artifact guard rejects standalone BROKER_REQUEST_ACTIVITY_REVIEW JSON even when renamed. Supplied hashes remain local consistency references, not signatures or encryption.

Both journal snapshots are replayed again after computation and any changed head/content refuses the review. Missing histories remain absent and cannot imply agreement. This is sampled cross-database rechecking, not an atomic two-database transaction, execution lock, reserved admission, source attestation or detection of tail deletion/ABA changes by external writers. Existing checked marker and lookup guards retain all current-head, cooldown, pending ticket, capacity, caller-admission and independent final-IO permission requirements. No actual broker submission path is enabled.

The Accounts request-recovery fixture now additionally constructs an original request and two explicit fills in temporary journals, verifies matching terms without writes and catches an offsetting individual-price violation. UI validates the fixture's positive checks and false authority flags, labels fee/fund verification unavailable, suppresses private failures and retains practical black/purple beginner help. Existing rendered browser fixtures exercise this explanation; local controller checks alone do not establish a rendered/native pass. The sixteen-profile suite now makes this evidence mandatory in the activity profile without replacing existing activity/source/fee assertions. Tests include BUY/SELL bounds, incorrect leaves with consistent supplied chains, stale heads, missing/foreign histories, missing original observation, changed snapshot status, incomplete fills, untracked orders, equal-time conflict, concurrent head changes and fresh-process immutable replay.

## v0.147 explicit paced paper BTC asset fields and Windows smoke evidence

Accounts adds Read Paper BTC Rules. It requires the expected account UUID, separate confirmation and an already saved Windows paper key pair. Origin-first bounded POST `/api/execution/alpaca-paper/local-credentials/asset-rules` accepts only expected_account_id and confirm_read_only_paper; no browser path, URL, clock, symbol or transport can be supplied. Its reader performs exactly three fixed-host GETs: account, `/v2/assets/BTCUSD`, account. Stable expected account fields are required. It retains only allowlisted asset UUID, canonical BTC/USD symbol, crypto class, active/inactive status, strict tradable/fractionable booleans and positive bounded reported minimum size/trade increment/price_increment fields. Unknown/private asset attributes are discarded. Account status/permission or inactive/nontradable/fractional issues remain review reasons. No positions, order, fee or funding action is requested.

This shares the same per-account managed private pacing journal as Read Paper with Pacing, including OS ownership, pre-IO committed reservation, success cooldown, authentication/rate/error/unknown treatment, clock/permission checkpoints and final saved-pair OS readback. Bad/unknown metadata after IO leaves retained pending evidence; no retry or deletion occurs. Removing keys during IO suppresses acknowledgment. External saved-pair replacement after successful provider reads also suppresses acknowledgment without erasing already committed pacing. Other/no journals, original unpaced account reads and other hosts remain outside this coordination; it is not a provider-wide quota. Key permissions, asset entitlement, current metadata/constraint semantics, fees, funds, routing/live and release authority remain unverified/false. Never turn reported values into accepted order rounding or a tradable authorization. No cache or plan promotion is added.

The explicit control has practical bottom-right black/purple help, shares busy/generation/removal fencing, clears earlier inventory exports, validates false authority and numeric fields and provides no inventory download. Browser interception and actual-controller tests cover consent, coalescing, promoted/malformed replies, stale-result clearing and removal races. The inventory trial keeps its original12 GET checks and now additionally requires the three-GET asset treatment plus cross-reader durable cooldown; real-loopback HTTP checks the new route's denied confirmation/origin/unknown-URL paths without provider access. Standalone READ_ONLY_PAPER_ASSET_RULES reports include private account identity and are blocked by the public artifact guard even when renamed. Actual tests use injected HTTPS/vault and disposable journals only.

Primary provider references checked2026-10-18: https://docs.alpaca.markets/us/reference/get-v2-assets-symbol_or_asset_id documents paper asset lookup and legacy BTCUSD path notation; https://docs.alpaca.markets/us/docs/crypto-trading documents crypto asset fields. https://alpaca.markets/support/alpaca-crypto-coin-pair-faq describes dynamic minimums and increment semantics. These references do not constitute provider-account acceptance, a stable tick-size contract or permission to send orders; independent semantics/eligibility review remains open.

## v0.148 bounded next-original-ID recovery

`BrokerRequestJournal.lookup_review(evaluated_at=...)` passively replays one bounded snapshot and selects the earliest retained due unresolved request, then least attempts/client ID as deterministic ties. It skips prepared-unsent, terminal and attempt-capped requests without deleting or rearming them; skipped terminal/capped history still needs separate review. Account pending lookup, authentication/review block, clock regression, shared lookup cooldown or insufficient aggregate recovery-event headroom prevents selection. Missing history creates no directories/files. Returned candidate/head/time/counts/capacity are diagnostics: lookup_admitted, admission_reserved, automatic continuation/retry/submission and financial/release flags remain false.

`nexus.paper_recovery_step.recover_next` and `recover_next_saved` perform one separately confirmed original-ID lookup through the existing fixed paper GET coordinator. The exact selection head is checked against read-only original terms before provider IO and again under SQLite write ownership by the optional expected_head on begin_lookup_checked. A concurrent append refuses before a provider request rather than silently choosing another target. There is no scan loop, automatic retry, submission/cancellation, callback-driven financial authority or new application path/URL input. Saved reads retain credential-epoch/final-OS-pair checks, clock continuity and optional explicit account pacing. A failure may already retain pending evidence; never automatically retry, erase it or infer no fill. Legacy callers omitting the new head reference keep existing callback/crash-recovery semantics; event schema, original IDs, caps and replay are unchanged.

The explicit source CLI is `python -m nexus.paper_recovery_step --expected-account UUID --journal PRIVATE_REQUEST_JOURNAL --pacing PRIVATE_PACING_JOURNAL --review-only`. It reads local selection/pacing without opening the vault/provider and preserves missing storage. For one confirmed saved-Windows-key read, replace --review-only with --confirm-read-only-paper. Both paths must be distinct and outside the checkout; original request history must already exist and be eligible. Review may show a local candidate while the separately reported pacing state still blocks admission. No key/secret arguments, environment-key fallback, default journal, recurring schedule or automatically started worker is added. Output includes private client IDs/head/account scope; keep it outside Git. Fixed CLI usage/failure messages never echo arguments, paths or transport details. Public artifact rules reject standalone selection, review and recovery-step JSON even when renamed.

The existing Accounts offline recovery check now demonstrates two separately invoked next-request steps, each with one original-ID lookup, plus passive cooldown preservation and a changed-head denial before IO. UI validates the fixture's checks and false authority flags, clears failed/stale results and uses the existing practical black/purple help. The mandatory pacing release profile requires all selection, single-step, cooldown/no-write and head-fence evidence, without changing its original pacing/submission tests or the sixteen-profile count. All fixture account/HTTPS/vault data is injected or temporary; no actual provider/vault is accessed by runtime fixtures. Unit checks include fairness, skipped states, pending/auth/rate/clock/headroom blocks before vault, persistent attempt caps, terminal observation, in-flight head changes, interrupted IO, lost result return, final saved-key readback, explicit consent and actual CLI missing-state/privacy behavior.

## v0.148.1 shared recovery review and process-death trials

`paper_recovery_step.review_next` composes bounded read-only request and optional exact-account pacing snapshots, then samples both again to reject changes during composition. It reports pending/authentication-or-review/clock/cooldown/session-cap blocks before saved-key access. Both explicit recovery entry points use this preflight; separately confirmed reads still recheck keys, time, local OS ownership, pacing admission and the exact request head under write ownership. Local clear checks do not reserve either journal or grant provider/trading permission; sequential snapshots cannot establish an atomic cross-database state or detect valid-tail/ABA replacement. Missing histories stay absent. The existing private --review-only CLI now includes these combined reasons while preserving its selection/pacing fields. Actual output contains private journal/account scope and must remain outside Git.

`python -m nexus.paper_recovery_drill` accepts no production storage or credentials and creates five disposable treatments: death after inserting lookup begin before commit, after begin commit, after inserting lookup result before commit, after result commit before return/pacing completion, and after pacing-result commit before return. Each uses injected GET-only account/original-ID/account responses and two fresh-process reviews. The drill requires exact retained attempts, partial-fill observation only after result commit, preserved original submission uncertainty, the expected pending/completed pacing state, real precommit rollback sidecars, unchanged fingerprints of both SQLite main files and every rollback/WAL/shared-memory sidecar, reacquired kernel ownership and no automatic follow-up. Pending request or pacing state and completed-session cooldown must deny a further explicit saved-key step before vault/provider access; released OS ownership never clears retained pending evidence.

The existing sixteen-profile suite now requires all five cases and all false authority flags in its pacing profile. Public drill output contains aggregate fixture counts/checks only; child errors expose fixed codes, not paths, raw stderr or keys. Hooks are local disposable-instance replacements in child processes and never production callbacks, startup work, a background recovery worker or order transport. This validates local commit/replay behavior, not actual provider outcomes, broker-wide pacing, native Windows/profile/ACL acceptance, source/fee/hold/funds reconciliation, sustained strategy performance, funding/live/cloud or independent/owner1.0 acceptance. ## v0.150 combined private account evidence review

`nexus.paper_account_evidence_review.review` composes the v0.149 accepted original-request/fill review and the accepted inventory-pair review. It requires distinct private journals, pinned request/activity heads, complete accepted history with no legacy baseline or unbound continuation, identical account/activity/pacing/acceptance heads across both components, retained false authority flags and final unchanged journal replay. It detaches and validates the supplied manifests before composition. Missing or promoted component bindings refuse; individual limit/quantity/identity blockers remain separate from gross-only balance differences. A boundary fill keeps arithmetic unavailable. Zero retained fee records or zero gross differences never prove zero actual fees, verified holds, spendable funds, account acceptance or profitability. No gross comparison is called P/L.

Source CLI: `python -m nexus.paper_account_evidence_review --expected-account UUID --request-journal PRIVATE_REQUESTS --pacing PRIVATE_PACING --activity-store PRIVATE_ACTIVITY --acceptance-store PRIVATE_ACCEPTANCE --request-head REQUEST_SHA --activity-head ACTIVITY_SHA --opening PRIVATE_OPENING_JSON --closing PRIVATE_CLOSING_JSON`. All six paths must be distinct and outside the checkout. Optional --opening-encrypted and --closing-encrypted reuse bounded private passphrase prompts and in-memory decoding without plaintext files. Output remains private plaintext financial data. Unknown arguments and failures return fixed codes without echoing paths, input bodies, keys or raw transport errors. The public artifact guard rejects standalone PAPER_ACCOUNT_EVIDENCE_BUNDLE_REVIEW reports even when renamed. No UI/API control, provider/vault access, journal/ledger writes or execution permission is added.

The sixteen-profile suite requires a temporary two-fill bundle, exact component heads, preserved gross differences, unchanged repeated review and no fee/funds/routing authority. Focused tests include offsetting individual-price violations, balance disagreement, boundary-window fills, stale/missing/mixed heads, changed request/pacing histories, missing false-authority fields, missing storage, journal aliases, network/vault-silent fresh CLI, encrypted-input no-write behavior and private failure suppression. Sequential sampled replays are not atomic cross-database ownership, source signatures or detection of valid-tail/ABA changes. Preserve all v0.149 accepted-history work and the v0.148.1 recovery preflight/five-boundary crash drill. ## v0.152 durable request-origin binding and historical replay

New request-derived collections use acceptance record profile `nexus.paper-activity-acceptance-record.v3`, adding exact request_head_sha256 and request_orders_sha256 fields to the existing account/content/receipt/pacing/activity references. The order digest covers the sorted canonical order map and account digest. The same source guards and optional precommit check remain required. Existing file-input collections stay v2; the new reader accepts strict v2/v3 records in the same retained chain without migration or rewriting. Older readers reject v3 instead of ignoring fields; use the matching/newer source for these private histories. Storage/event/record caps, source request schema and receipt schema are unchanged. No profiles grant broker source, fill/fee/hold/funds, order or release authority.

`BrokerRequestJournal.observations_at_heads` accepts at most128 digest references, opens existing storage read-only, starts one SQLite read transaction and validates the entire bounded current journal before reconstructing requested historical observations. A corrupt later event refuses even if an earlier requested prefix was valid. Missing/unknown heads are explicit, returned state is detached, and absent history remains absent. Prefix output contains private original IDs and has its own public-artifact rejection mode. It cannot attest provider source, prevent valid-tail/ABA replacement or establish distributed/atomic cross-journal ownership. No history is truncated, repaired, rearmed or rewritten.

The accepted original-request/activity review now re-derives every v3 origin from its recorded historical prefix and compares canonical original orders and the exact retained receipt to the persisted order digest. Current request-head checks still apply separately; a later prepared request can leave current agreement blocked while the earlier collection's origin remains replayable. The combined account-evidence review exposes bound, legacy-unbound and replayed-prefix counts. Old v2 history remains usable for its previous local-consistency scope, with zero inferred request origins; mixed history cannot claim all origins are bound. Three-journal status replays the persisted order-input digest but explicitly leaves request_origin_history_verified false because no request history was supplied to that status function. A digest or consistent local rewrite is not a provider signature or proof of collection causality.

The mandatory collection release profile now requires persisted origin heads, historical-prefix replay after a later append and network/vault-silent fresh-process origin replay. Tests include missing/unobserved prefixes, rehashed substituted references/digests, corrupt suffixes, bounded invalid reference sets, detached state, missing/foreign histories, strict v3 shapes, mixed v2/v3 records and cold replay without storage changes. The new-source saved offline report must be regenerated; prior reports lack these mandatory checks and cannot certify this source. Runtime UI, startup and order routes are unchanged. Encrypted observation inputs still decrypt only in memory after hidden prompts; no plaintext file is created by either review mode.

## v0.158 institutional design and execution-quality foundation

User direction now explicitly includes studying public institutional mechanics: Bloomberg AIM/PORT/EMSX, FlexTRADER, State Street Charles River, Iress, Itiviti/Tbricks, quantitative funds/HFT and IBKR. Read INSTITUTIONAL_DESIGN.md before choosing the next architecture change. That dated document preserves primary-source links, a platform-to-Nexus map, a practical ordered sequence and QuickFIX/SBE/CppTrader C++ candidates with their reviewed license distinctions. No proprietary source, private fund data, copied implementation or new dependency was imported. Avoid a wholesale C++ rewrite before profiling; preserve licensing notices and pin/review exact upstream revisions before any future adoption.

The target is parallel bounded council research outside a deterministic execution loop, expiring reviewed policy proposals, independent account-wide risk/stop/reservation checks, one execution owner per account, durable event-driven lifecycle and execution-cost feedback. Current learned council output remains advisory, current fixed-policy virtual execution unchanged and broker order routing disconnected. Do not evade provider limits with multiple clients or describe institution-style software as institution-free market access. Nanosecond timestamp precision is not clock synchronization, measured brokerage latency or a promise of nanosecond fills. More trades do not prove positive net edge or exponential growth.

Original nexus/execution_quality.py now supplies a bounded pure offline BTC/USD review: canonical unique execution IDs, explicit decimal prices/quantities/USD fee debits or None, same-domain-assumed integer nanoseconds, decision-benchmark gross shortfall, delay/arrival-spread/post-arrival-residual decomposition, supplied-fee totals/bps and separate unfilled end-mark opportunity cost. Positive signed shortfall is worse than the benchmark; a negative value is not realized profit. Missing fees stay unknown; a zero supplied fee never attests actual coverage. Missing end marks leave partial-order opportunity unknown; no-fill cases do not become successful zero-cost executions. Rebates/non-USD fees, causal impact, queue position, actual clock domains, fee/fill/source coverage and broker/account/order bindings remain unsupported/unverified. The function performs no network/vault/file/journal/ledger IO or routing. Decimal arithmetic uses a bounded local96-digit context and fixed rounding, not caller-selected rounding or float timestamps.

Matching records deduplicate without changing normalized input identity; conflicts, unsupported scopes, overfills, crossed quotes, out-of-interval fills and malformed/unbounded types refuse with fixed codes. ## v0.159 exact-head retained paper execution-quality review

`nexus/paper_execution_quality_review.py` now derives the selected original request quantity/side and every retained explicit priced fill directly from locally accepted request/activity/pacing/acceptance histories. It reuses complete accepted-history replay, original individual limit/quantity checks and request-origin handling, checks the exact supplied current request/activity heads and common account, then repeats accepted replay and snapshots after arithmetic. A changed history fails without retry/repair. Selected client/broker/payload identities, quote-benchmark digest, exact cooperating heads and quality-input digest are bound in the private report. REST activity IDs are explicitly local surrogate identities, never claimed to be verified streaming execution IDs. No fill is priced from cumulative averages, and no fill is silently excluded to fit a favorable benchmark interval.

The bounded benchmark schema has exactly decision_mid, decision_at_ns, arrival_bid, arrival_ask, arrival_at_ns, end_at_ns and unfilled_mark. Decimal prices/mark are strings; nanosecond instants must be bounded integers. Quotes, clock domains and original request-event timing remain supplied/unverified. Canonical retained RFC3339 fill times are converted by integer calendar arithmetic, retaining nanoseconds/offset equivalence without float timestamps. Every fee input is None: retained fee rows are counted but never associated by a date/reference/residual match. Explicit fees and all fee-inclusive/whole-order totals stay unknown. No complete received coverage, funds, account, benchmark, causal impact, profit, latency or routing acceptance follows.

`NexusOperative.exe --paper-tool quality-review --help` is the sixth fixed packaged command (v0.159+). The CLI requires the private account ID, four distinct journals, exact current request/activity fingerprints, original client order ID and a fifth distinct private benchmark file. It rejects source/install paths and aliases before reads; benchmark JSON is bounded to4096bytes, refuses duplicate keys/nonfinite tokens, and is read again before acknowledgment. Private output contains identifiers, hashes and amounts and must stay outside Git. No password/key/provider access, default-data/desktop startup, journal/ledger writes or automatic continuation is added. This is optional operator tooling, not another first-run setup checkbox.

Standalone/installed smoke now executes nine child commands across six allowlisted tools and requires the original-request-derived quality result, unknown fees, false authority flags and unchanged private files. The existing capacity release profile additionally requires a separate synthetic accepted-history quality fixture; it is not included in timed benchmark treatments. ## v0.160 explicit fixed-host BTC quote observation foundation

The internal `nexus/paper_quote_observation.py` reader makes exactly three GETs after separate explicit confirmation: paper account, `https://data.alpaca.markets/v1beta3/crypto/us/latest/quotes?symbols=BTC%2FUSD`, then the same paper account. The generic existing paper get(url) allowlist remains unchanged; only a no-URL-argument method admits this one market-data endpoint. Both paths share the existing redirect-disabled, exact response-URL, bounded body/header/time, permission/clock and <=4-GET session transport. Shared-transport refactoring preserves account/activity/asset/recovery guards. No live trading host, arbitrary URL/location/symbol, order method, retries or new network dependency is admitted.

Official documentation reviewed2026-10-18: https://docs.alpaca.markets/us/docs/authentication confirms paper-account access to the data.alpaca.markets host; https://docs.alpaca.markets/us/reference/cryptolatestquotes-1 documents the latest-quote endpoint and locations; https://docs.alpaca.markets/us/docs/real-time-crypto-pricing-data documents crypto quote fields and RFC3339 nanosecond timestamps. These public specifications are not actual account/schema/entitlement acceptance. Only fixed Alpaca US BTC/USD quote fields are admitted; foreign symbols, unknown fields, crossed prices, malformed sizes, stale/future timestamps and account disagreement refuse. Zero reported size stays review-only, not executable liquidity. Complete request/schema/source/location acceptance remains open.

The new reader requires a caller-selected private account-bound PaperReadPacing journal. Source/install paths are rejected before storage/keys. Successful reads share the retained60-second cooldown with inventory, activity, asset and recovery operations using that exact journal. Failed malformed reads preserve pending uncertainty; HTTP/rate/auth outcomes retain existing conservative rules. This is an explicit acceptance diagnostic, not a high-frequency polling feed or broker-wide/distributed quota proof. Existing public discovery/research refresh settings are unchanged.

Exchange timestamps retain all nine fractional digits; their integer-nanosecond representation is serialized as decimal text to avoid JavaScript number rounding. Local receipt/complete/finalized samples remain float seconds, explicitly not synchronized nanosecond clocks or actual order arrival. Freshness is rechecked after the final account GET and after durable pacing completion; a delayed commit cannot acknowledge a stale quote. Matching normalized account fields across reads are sequential stability only, not an atomic account/quote snapshot or entitlement proof.

`paper_credentials.inspect_quote_saved` is internal and separately confirmed. Invalid/corrupt/foreign/private-path scopes and retained pacing blocks are checked before vault access; actual admission is rechecked under existing ownership. Saved-key mutation epochs fence every IO checkpoint, and final OS-store readback suppresses return if another process replaces credentials. Revoked/incomplete reads preserve pending history; a completed but revoked/stale return never clears or rewrites its committed pacing result. No credentials/raw responses/private account fields are returned.

This batch adds no UI control, API route or EXE command, no default storage/startup network read, no automatic quote retention and no original request/decision/arrival-event benchmark binding. The output remains private account-bound financial data, with source/clock/benchmark/fees/venue/liquidity/funds/routing/release flags false; public artifact scanning rejects its report mode. ## v0.161 private original-request quote retention

`nexus.paper_quote_store.PaperQuoteStore` is an internal offline foundation, with no default path, network/vault call, UI/API/EXE entry point or startup capture. It accepts a supplied v0.160-shaped observation and binds only its allowlisted normalized scope to an exact original request head, original client ID, retained plan digest and payload digest. The source label is copied unverified; even a valid hash does not prove the reader was called or that the quote was contemporaneous with a decision/order event. No supplied observation can promote any source, clock, benchmark, fee, funds, routing, money or release authority flag. All remain false. No execution-quality benchmark is generated or accepted automatically.

Both files must be distinct private paths outside the checkout/install tree. SQLite uses regular single-link bounded DELETE-mode files with WAL/sidecar rejection, FULL-synchronous transactions, a4MiB page/file cap,128 retained observations and8KiB record bounds. Public JSON artifact checks reject both history and receipt modes. Files are private financial information, not encrypted by this store; use an appropriate encrypted private volume and independently accept native profile/ACL/key recovery. Never publish reports, copy into Git, prune, rebaseline, convert WAL or clear history to regain admission.

Every review validates the complete current original-request tail and complete quote chain. Each retained request head must be a validated historical prefix containing that original PREPARE; immutable retained plan/payload digests must agree. Later request appends preserve earlier quote bindings. Capture time remains sampled local float seconds; source exchange nanoseconds are exact decimal text. Record replay reproduces normalization/freshness against the old sampled read times only: an old stored quote never becomes current executable data. No clock-domain synchronization, request-event causality, actual order arrival, venue/liquidity entitlement or broker latency is inferred.

Append requires explicit current request and quote heads, replays again under the quote write transaction, and checks original history and caller revocation before commit. Exact duplicates reuse identity without adding rows, including at capacity. Cap exhaustion refuses without eviction. Request change or revocation after commit suppresses acknowledgment but retains the committed record for explicit review. Separate SQLite files are sampled, not atomic; hashes do not authenticate data, exclude valid-tail deletion/ABA/path races, prove distributed ownership or reserve execution. Missing status reads remain absent. Failed first creation can leave an uninitialized file requiring review; no automatic repair is provided.

The new store is deliberately not a collection coordinator and does not consume or change any pacing journal. Existing complete accepted-activity history still requires one activity receipt per pacing session. Do not append quote reads into that accepted history or erase unbound sessions. A reviewed typed central read budget, retained quote-to-pacing causality, explicit capture flow, exact original-event timing and authorized broker/native acceptance remain next. Mandatory inspection_inventory evidence exercises temporary supplied retention/prefix replay with aggregate booleans only; injected/supplied tests are not actual account/provider acceptance.

Rough implementation remains about40% (30–45%, low confidence),0/9 fully accepted major stages. Connected broker-paper, sustained real-feed trials, verified fees/holds/funds, secure provider-hosted funding, governed live integration, authenticated computer-off cloud, independent security/risk and owner1.0 approval remain open.

Real-process death tests cover both sides of the quote commit. A retained rollback sidecar makes both status and append refuse before SQLite can recover or alter it; post-commit death retains the new record for explicit read-only replay. Never delete a rollback sidecar to regain admission.

## v0.162 explicit quote capture and offline recovery

`nexus.paper_quote_capture` adds the seventh allowlisted EXE tool, `--paper-tool quote-capture`. Normal no-argument desktop startup is unchanged. Status needs no saved keys, network, directory/database creation or repair. A read requires separate `--confirm-read-only-paper`, an existing original request/client ID, exact current request/quote/pacing heads and three distinct private paths outside source/install directories. It makes the existing three fixed GETs only; it never prepares, marks, submits, cancels or retries an order. No bank/funding/live or Engage authority follows.

Preflight replays complete histories and checks original terms, caps and cooldown before touching saved credentials. The reader rechecks the expected pacing head after acquiring its local read lease and before reserving/IO. Existing redirect, fixed-host/body/time, account, clock and saved-key mutation safeguards remain. After the reader completes its durable result, capture reacquires pacing ownership, rejects any intervening transition, and holds ownership through quote retention/cleanup. This is a checked handoff, not an uninterrupted lease or distributed reservation. Uncooperating writers, ABA/path races and other hosts remain outside the local contract.

Existing supplied v1 quotes remain replayable without rewriting. New capture records use v2 with the selected local pacing-path/account scope digest and exact before/after heads. Offline review requires precisely one v2 record for every retained successful pacing session in order, plus original-request historical-prefix agreement. Supplied v1 records, pending/failed/interrupted/unbound sessions, wrong copied-path scope or mismatched transitions block further capture. Never invent a missing receipt, clear uncertainty, delete/rebaseline history or switch journals to bypass review/provider limits. Captures do not belong in the existing accepted-activity pacing history: its one-activity-receipt-per-session contract remains unchanged. A reviewed typed central budget is still required for mixed operations; a dedicated diagnostic path is not proof of provider-wide quota coordination.

The saved-pair consumer keeps the captured epoch and repeated OS-store readback checks inside the credential module through local retention. It shares a permission guard for original/quote history during the read, uses post-lock checks before quote commit, and suppresses acknowledgment after key/history revocation while preserving already committed evidence. A completed provider read followed by failed/no quote retention stays explicitly unbound; a committed quote with lost return remains reviewable. Full process-death fixtures cover both outcomes. This is local content agreement, never provider causality or source attestation.

Capture stores the reader's sampled timing; historical replay never makes an old quote fresh for execution. Exact exchange nanoseconds and a recent latest quote do not establish a decision/arrival event, clock synchronization, source/location entitlement, executable liquidity, fees, latency, spendable funds or profitability. No benchmark is generated/promoted automatically. All authority flags remain false. Reports and journals are private financial information; the store itself is not encryption. Native private-volume/ACL/key recovery, actual source/schema/account acceptance and reviewed event timing remain required.

The common standalone/installed smoke now runs eleven actual child commands across seven tools, adding a bound synthetic quote-status replay and refusal of an unconfirmed quote read. All disposable fixture files are prepared before byte-snapshot comparison; child private JSON/paths stay out of aggregate evidence. The inspection_inventory profile requires actual coordinator/reader/retention fixture checks with injected transport and no actual provider/vault access. This is not actual Windows/provider acceptance. Existing beginner tabs/help, engine/throttle and disconnected order adapters remain unchanged.

Next: accept this explicit flow on separately authorized paper-account/native-profile data; review source/location/clock domains and original event times; integrate typed provider-wide budgeting before mixed production collection.
